cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/oga && cat > src/sheet.py <<'EOF'
"""Tiny sheet helpers: nearest-neighbour zoom, labelled rows."""
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw, ImageFont
import numpy as np
try:
    FONT = ImageFont.truetype('/System/Library/Fonts/Helvetica.ttc', 14)
    FONT_S = ImageFont.truetype('/System/Library/Fonts/Helvetica.ttc', 11)
except Exception:
    FONT = FONT_S = ImageFont.load_default()


def to_img(a):
    if isinstance(a, Image.Image):
        return a.convert('RGB')
    a = np.asarray(a)
    if a.dtype != np.uint8:
        a = (np.clip(a, 0, 1) * 255 + .5).astype(np.uint8)
    return Image.fromarray(a).convert('RGB')


def zoom(a, k):
    im = to_img(a)
    return im.resize((im.width * k, im.height * k), Image.NEAREST)


def fit(a, w=None, h=None, nearest=False):
    im = to_img(a)
    s = min((w or 1e9) / im.width, (h or 1e9) / im.height)
    return im.resize((max(1, round(im.width * s)), max(1, round(im.height * s))),
                     Image.NEAREST if nearest else Image.LANCZOS)


def grid(cells, cols, labels=None, pad=8, title=None, bg=(240, 238, 232)):
    """cells: list of PIL images (any sizes); returns a sheet with labels under each."""
    cw = max(c.width for c in cells); ch = max(c.height for c in cells)
    rows = (len(cells) + cols - 1) // cols
    th = 26 if title else 0; lh = 18 if labels else 0
    W = cols * (cw + pad) + pad; H = th + rows * (ch + lh + pad) + pad
    out = Image.new('RGB', (W, H), bg); d = ImageDraw.Draw(out)
    if title:
        d.text((pad, 5), title, fill=(20, 20, 20), font=FONT)
    for i, c in enumerate(cells):
        x = pad + (i % cols) * (cw + pad); y = th + pad + (i // cols) * (ch + lh + pad)
        out.paste(c, (x, y))
        if labels:
            d.text((x, y + c.height + 2), labels[i], fill=(30, 30, 30), font=FONT_S)
    return out


def area(a, w):
    im = to_img(a); h = max(1, round(im.height * w / im.width))
    return im.resize((w, h), Image.BOX)

Write: books/oga/src/ground.py
"""地塗り (ground laying) in pixels.

Oga (p.46-47): ground laying is the most important process. It settles light direction, overall
colour and atmosphere, and he brings it "as close to finished as possible" at the lowest level of
detail. On wet paper the paints bleed (にじみ) and blur (ぼかし) into each other without mixing fully
(混ざり切らない), and the unevenness of the paint (塗りムラ) reads as light and texture.

Pixel translation:
  * a small set of paints (k colours, the "few colours freely" of p.41), chosen for the picture;
  * the picture laid at a coarse cell size (the width of a flat brush);
  * no averaging between paints: every pixel is one paint, and a soft coarse weight field is
    sampled with a clumped noise, so two paints interleave in patches (unevenness) and bleed
    across a boundary in ragged tongues (wet edges) instead of a straight cut or a smooth blend.
"""
import numpy as np
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter, zoom as ndzoom
from okl import rgb_to_oklab, oklab_to_rgb


def clumped_noise(shape, scale=2.0, seed=0, octaves=((1.0, 1.0), (0.35, 0.5))):
    """uniform-ish [0,1) noise with patches ~scale px (gaussian-filtered white noise, rank-normalised)."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n = np.zeros(shape)
    for amp, sm in octaves:
        n += amp * gaussian_filter(rng.standard_normal(shape), scale * sm)
    r = n.ravel().argsort().argsort().reshape(shape)
    return (r + 0.5) / r.size


def kmeans(X, k, iters=25, seed=0, w=None):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    w = np.ones(len(X)) if w is None else w
    # k-means++ init
    c = [X[rng.choice(len(X), p=w / w.sum())]]
    for _ in range(k - 1):
        d = np.min(((X[:, None] - np.array(c)[None]) ** 2).sum(-1), 1) * w
        c.append(X[rng.choice(len(X), p=d / d.sum())])
    c = np.array(c)
    for _ in range(iters):
        lab = np.argmin(((X[:, None] - c[None]) ** 2).sum(-1), 1)
        for j in range(k):
            m = lab == j
            if m.any():
                c[j] = (X[m] * w[m, None]).sum(0) / w[m].sum()
    return c


def choose_paints(lab, k, seed=0, Lweight=1.6):
    """pick k paints from an OKLab image; L weighted up so value structure wins over hue noise."""
    X = lab.reshape(-1, 3).copy()
    sc = np.array([Lweight, 1, 1])
    c = kmeans(X * sc, k, seed=seed) / sc
    return c[np.argsort(c[:, 0])]                       # dark -> light


def paint_weights(lab, paints, sharp=40.0):
    """soft membership of each pixel in each paint (softmax of -distance)."""
    d = ((lab[..., None, :] - paints[None, None]) ** 2 * np.array([1.6, 1, 1])).sum(-1)
    w = np.exp(-sharp * (d - d.min(-1, keepdims=True)) / max(d.mean(), 1e-9))
    return w / w.sum(-1, keepdims=True)


def ground(rgb, W=320, cell=4, k=10, bleed=1.0, clump=1.6, seed=0, paints=None, sharp=6.0):
    """Lay the ground of an image.

    rgb   : float image 0..1 (any size), the subject (photo, plate, render, or a planned scene)
    W     : output width in pixels
    cell  : flat-brush width in px; the picture is decided at this resolution
    k     : number of paints
    bleed : how far (in cells) paints bleed into each other at boundaries
    clump : patch size of the interleaving noise, px (unevenness)
    sharp : how decisively a cell takes its main paint (low = more half-mixed interleave)
    returns (index image HxW, paints OKLab kx3)
    """
    from PIL import Image
    im = Image.fromarray((np.clip(rgb, 0, 1) * 255).astype(np.uint8))
    H = round(im.height * W / im.width)
    Hc, Wc = max(2, round(H / cell)), max(2, round(W / cell))
    coarse = np.asarray(im.resize((Wc, Hc), Image.BOX), np.float64) / 255
    lab_c = rgb_to_oklab(coarse)
    if paints is None:
        paints = choose_paints(lab_c, k, seed)
    wc = paint_weights(lab_c, paints, sharp)                       # Hc,Wc,k
    # bleed: soften the coarse weights by `bleed` cells, then upsample smoothly to full res
    if bleed > 0:
        wc = np.stack([gaussian_filter(wc[..., j], bleed * 0.6) for j in range(len(paints))], -1)
    wf = ndzoom(wc, (H / Hc, W / Wc, 1), order=1)[:H, :W]
    wf /= wf.sum(-1, keepdims=True)
    # sample: cumulative weights against one clumped noise -> one paint per pixel, patches not grain
    u = clumped_noise((H, W), clump, seed)
    cum = np.cumsum(wf, -1)
    idx = (u[..., None] > cum).sum(-1)
    return np.clip(idx, 0, len(paints) - 1), paints


def render(idx, paints):
    return oklab_to_rgb(paints[idx])

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/oga && python3 - <<'EOF'

s=s[:i]+'''def split_paint(p, amount=0.03, warm=np.array([0.55, 0.83])):
    """the two paints a mass colour was 'not fully mixed' from: same L, pushed apart along a
    warm/cool axis in (a,b). Their average is the mass colour."""
    d = warm / np.linalg.norm(warm)
    return p + np.r_[0, d * amount], p - np.r_[0, d * amount]


def ground(rgb, W=320, cell=4, k=10, bleed=1.0, clump=1.6, seed=0, paints=None,
           gamma=4.0, mix=0.03, mix_clump=None, mix_share=0.5):
    """Lay the ground of an image.

    rgb    : float image 0..1 (any size): the subject (photo, plate, render, or a planned scene)
    W      : output width in pixels
    cell   : flat-brush width in px; the picture is decided at this resolution
    k      : number of paints (mass colours)
    bleed  : how far (in cells) paints bleed into each other at boundaries
    clump  : patch size of the boundary noise, px (ragged wet edges)
    gamma  : decisiveness of a mass; high = paints bleed only in a boundary band
    mix    : OKLab distance of the two half-mixed components of each paint (0 = flat paint)
    mix_clump : patch size of the unevenness inside a mass (default 1.5 cells)
    returns (index image HxW into palette, palette OKLab (2k)x3 ; index 2j,2j+1 = paint j's halves)
    """
    from PIL import Image
    im = Image.fromarray((np.clip(rgb, 0, 1) * 255).astype(np.uint8))
    H = round(im.height * W / im.width)
    Hc, Wc = max(2, round(H / cell)), max(2, round(W / cell))
    coarse = np.asarray(im.resize((Wc, Hc), Image.BOX), np.float64) / 255
    lab_c = rgb_to_oklab(coarse)
    if paints is None:
        paints = choose_paints(lab_c, k, seed)
    k = len(paints)
    d = ((lab_c[..., None, :] - paints[None, None]) ** 2 * np.array([1.6, 1, 1])).sum(-1)
    lab_hard = np.argmin(d, -1)
    wc = np.eye(k)[lab_hard]
    if bleed > 0:
        wc = np.stack([gaussian_filter(wc[..., j], bleed * 0.6) for j in range(k)], -1)
    wf = ndzoom(wc, (H / Hc, W / Wc, 1), order=1)[:H, :W]
    wf = np.clip(wf, 0, None) ** gamma
    wf /= wf.sum(-1, keepdims=True)
    u = clumped_noise((H, W), clump, seed)
    idx = np.clip((u[..., None] > np.cumsum(wf, -1)).sum(-1), 0, k - 1)
    pal = np.concatenate([np.stack(split_paint(p, mix)) for p in paints])
    u2 = clumped_noise((H, W), mix_clump or 1.5 * cell, seed + 7)
    half = (u2 < mix_share).astype(int)
    return 2 * idx + half, pal


def render(idx, pal):
    return oklab_to_rgb(pal[idx])

"""Scene plans: named planes (masks) with a material and a depth order.

A plan is what Oga's pencil art setting gives him: which masses exist, what they are, what is in
front. For voxsim it comes from the simulator (material id, depth, normals). For photo tests I draw
it by hand as polygons (plans/*.py)."""
import importlib.util
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw


def load_plan(path):
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('plan', path)
    m = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(m)
    return m


def rasterize(planes, W, H):
    """-> plane id image (H,W) (index into planes; later polygons overwrite earlier)."""
    im = Image.new('L', (W * 4, H * 4), 0); d = ImageDraw.Draw(im)
    for i, (_, _, poly) in enumerate(planes):
        if poly is None:
            d.rectangle([0, 0, W * 4, H * 4], fill=i)
        else:
            d.polygon([(x * W * 4, y * H * 4) for x, y in poly], fill=i)
    return np.asarray(im.resize((W, H), Image.NEAREST))

def otsu1(x):
    h, e = np.histogram(x, 64); c = (e[:-1] + e[1:]) / 2
    w0 = np.cumsum(h); w1 = w0[-1] - w0
    m0 = np.cumsum(h * c) / np.maximum(w0, 1); m1 = (np.sum(h * c) - np.cumsum(h * c)) / np.maximum(w1, 1)
    return c[np.argmax(w0 * w1 * (m0 - m1) ** 2)]


def ground_from_plan(rgb, pid, cell=4, organise=1.2, rag=0.35, clump=1.4, mix=0.012, seed=0, nplanes=None):
    """Organised ground: each plane gets two paints, lit and shadow, and the lit/shadow shapes are
    decided at cell resolution and smoothed `organise` cells (the painter organising the crowd).

    rgb : subject image (colour source), any size
    pid : plane-id map at the OUTPUT size (H,W)
    rag : raggedness of the lit/shadow boundary (wet bleed), in units of the family field
    returns idx (H,W) into pal, pal OKLab, fam (H,W) 0 shadow / 1 lit
    """
    from PIL import Image
    H, W = pid.shape
    im = Image.fromarray((np.clip(rgb, 0, 1) * 255).astype(np.uint8))
    full = rgb_to_oklab(np.asarray(im.resize((W, H), Image.BOX), float) / 255)
    Hc, Wc = max(2, round(H / cell)), max(2, round(W / cell))
    Lc = rgb_to_oklab(np.asarray(im.resize((Wc, Hc), Image.BOX), float) / 255)[..., 0]
    Lc = gaussian_filter(Lc, organise)
    Lf = ndzoom(Lc, (H / Hc, W / Wc), order=1)[:H, :W]
    nplanes = nplanes or int(pid.max()) + 1
    u = clumped_noise((H, W), clump, seed) - 0.5
    fam = np.zeros((H, W), int); pal = []
    for p in range(nplanes):
        m = pid == p
        if not m.any():
            pal += [np.zeros(3)] * 4; continue
        t = otsu1(full[..., 0][m]) if m.sum() > 30 else full[..., 0][m].mean()
        spread = max(full[..., 0][m].std(), 0.02)
        f = (Lf - t) / spread + rag * 2 * u
        fam[m] = (f[m] > 0)
        for fv in (0, 1):
            mm = m & ((full[..., 0] > t) == bool(fv))
            c = full[mm].mean(0) if mm.sum() > 3 else full[m].mean(0)
            pal += list(split_paint(c, mix))
    pal = np.array(pal)
    half = (clumped_noise((H, W), 1.5 * cell, seed + 7) < 0.5).astype(int)
    idx = pid * 4 + fam * 2 + half
    return idx, pal, fam

"""A small procedural test world and a renderer to *fields* (not colours).

The Oga painter never sees colours from here, only what voxsim can give a painter:
  mat    material id per pixel      (SKY, FAR, ROCK, GRASS, FOREST, CONIFER, WATER, EARTH, SNOW)
  depth  metres from the eye
  nrm    world normal (x east, y up, z north)
  sun    direct light 0..1 (n.s times cast-shadow visibility)
  shadow cast-shadow mask (1 = in cast shadow)
  wx,wz  world position (for texture that sticks to the ground)
  crown  per-pixel crown id for trees (-1 if none), and crown-local normal

Views:
  landscape : eye ~1.7 m .. 40 m above ground, looking along +z, perspective (voxel-space columns)
  iso       : 30 degree parallel projection over a patch
The world: a valley meadow slope with a stream, forest and conifer stands, a lake, rock
outcrops, and far ridges as layered silhouettes (the book's far range, p.54)."""
import numpy as np
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter, map_coordinates

SKY, FAR, ROCK, GRASS, FOREST, CONIFER, WATER, EARTH, SNOW = range(9)
MAT_NAMES = ['sky', 'far', 'rock', 'grass', 'forest', 'conifer', 'water', 'earth', 'snow']


def fbm(shape, seed, scales=(64, 32, 16, 8, 4), amps=(1, .5, .25, .12, .06)):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    out = np.zeros(shape)
    for s, a in zip(scales, amps):
        out += a * gaussian_filter(rng.standard_normal(shape), s, mode='wrap') * s ** 0.5
    return out / np.abs(out).max()


class World:
    """heightfield on an N x N grid of `res` metres. z grows away from the default camera."""

    def __init__(self, N=1024, res=2.0, seed=0, kind='valley', snow=False, lake=False):
        self.N, self.res, self.seed, self.snow = N, res, seed, snow
        n = N
        z, x = np.mgrid[0:n, 0:n] * res
        self.X, self.Z = x, z
        cx = n * res / 2
        L = n * res
        # valley: floor rising gently away, walls rising left and right
        u = (x - cx) / (L / 2)
        base = 0.035 * z + 260 * np.abs(u) ** 1.6 + 30 * u
        base += 40 * fbm((n, n), seed, (96, 48, 24), (1, .5, .25))
        base += 6 * fbm((n, n), seed + 1, (12, 6, 3), (1, .5, .3))
        # a meadow bench on the right of the camera: flatten a band
        bench = np.exp(-((u - 0.18) / 0.16) ** 2) * np.exp(-((z - 700) / 500) ** 2)
        base = base * (1 - 0.5 * bench) + bench * (0.035 * z + 30) * 0.5
        # stream along the valley floor
        sx = cx - 60 + 70 * np.sin(z / 260 + seed)
        dstream = np.abs(x - sx)
        base -= 3.0 * np.exp(-(dstream / 6) ** 2)
        self.stream = dstream < 3.5
        if lake:
            lk = ((x - cx + 40) / 180) ** 2 + ((z - 1100) / 260) ** 2 < 1
            self.lake_level = np.percentile(base[lk], 40)
            base = np.where(lk, np.minimum(base, self.lake_level - 0.5), base)
            self.lake = lk & (base < self.lake_level)
        else:
            self.lake = np.zeros_like(self.stream)
        self.ground = base
        # slope
        gz, gx = np.gradient(base, res)
        self.slope = np.hypot(gx, gz)
        # vegetation: forest density field; forest on walls, meadow on bench/floor; rock on steep
        dens = fbm((n, n), seed + 2, (40, 20, 10), (1, .6, .3))
        forest = (dens + 0.9 * np.clip(np.abs(u) - 0.12, 0, 1) * 3 - 0.35 * bench) > 0.25
        forest &= self.slope < 1.1
        forest &= ~self.stream & ~self.lake
        # keep a clearing right in front of the camera
        forest &= ~((np.abs(x - cx - 10) < 90) & (z < 260))
        self.forest = forest
        self.rock = (self.slope > 0.95) & ~forest
        # canopy: crowns as bumps. conifers where colder/higher or on one side
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed + 3)
        conif_pref = fbm((n, n), seed + 4, (80, 40), (1, .5)) + 0.004 * (base - base.mean())
        self.conifer_zone = conif_pref > 0.0
        canopy = np.zeros((n, n)); crown_id = -np.ones((n, n), int)
        # scatter trees
        k = int(forest.sum() / (5.5 / res) ** 2 * 0.9)
        ys, xs = np.nonzero(forest)
        pick = rng.choice(len(ys), size=min(k, len(ys)), replace=False)
        self.trees = []
        for t, i in enumerate(pick):
            ty, tx = ys[i], xs[i]
            con = self.conifer_zone[ty, tx]
            h = rng.uniform(18, 42) if con else rng.uniform(12, 24)
            r = (h * rng.uniform(0.16, 0.22)) if con else (h * rng.uniform(0.3, 0.42))
            self.trees.append((tx * res, ty * res, base[ty, tx], h, r, con))
        self._paint_canopy(canopy, crown_id)
        self.canopy = canopy
        self.crown_id = crown_id
        self.top = base + canopy

    def _paint_canopy(self, canopy, crown_id):
        res = self.res; n = self.N
        for t, (x0, z0, g, h, r, con) in enumerate(self.trees):
            R = int(np.ceil(r / res)) + 1
            ix, iz = int(x0 / res), int(z0 / res)
            a, b = max(0, iz - R), min(n, iz + R + 1)
            c, d = max(0, ix - R), min(n, ix + R + 1)
            if a >= b or c >= d:
                continue
            zz, xx = np.mgrid[a:b, c:d] * res
            dd = np.hypot(xx - x0, zz - z0) / r
            if con:   # cone crown
                hh = h * np.clip(1 - dd, 0, 1) ** 1.0 + 0.25 * h * (dd < 1)
            else:     # dome crown
                hh = (0.55 * h + 0.45 * h * np.sqrt(np.clip(1 - dd ** 2, 0, 1))) * (dd < 1)
            sub = canopy[a:b, c:d]
            m = hh > sub
            sub[m] = hh[m]
            crown_id[a:b, c:d][m] = t

    def sample(self, arr, x, z, order=1):
        return map_coordinates(arr, [z / self.res, x / self.res], order=order, mode='nearest')

    def sun_shadow(self, sun, steps=160, step=4.0):
        """cast-shadow visibility on the grid for a sun direction (unit, y up). 1 lit, 0 shadow."""
        n = self.N
        top = self.top
        vis = np.ones((n, n))
        horiz = np.hypot(sun[0], sun[2]) + 1e-9
        dx, dz = sun[0] / horiz, sun[2] / horiz
        rise = sun[1] / horiz
        for k in range(1, steps + 1):
            d = k * step
            h = self.sample(top, self.X + dx * d, self.Z + dz * d)
            vis = np.minimum(vis, np.clip(1 - (h - (top + 0.5 + rise * d)) / 2.0, 0, 1))
        return vis


def normals_from(h, res):
    gz, gx = np.gradient(h, res)
    n = np.stack([-gx, np.ones_like(h), -gz], -1)
    return n / np.linalg.norm(n, axis=-1, keepdims=True)


def render_landscape(w, W=320, H=180, eye=(None, 30.0, 40.0), yaw=0.0, pitch=-4.0, fov=58.0,
                     sun=(0.6, 0.55, -0.55), far_ridges=True, zmax=None, seed=0):
    """voxel-space column renderer. eye = (x, height above ground, z)."""
    sun = np.asarray(sun, float); sun /= np.linalg.norm(sun)
    ex = w.N * w.res / 2 if eye[0] is None else eye[0]
    ez = eye[2]
    eg = w.sample(w.ground, np.array([ex]), np.array([ez]))[0]
    ey = eg + eye[1]
    f = (W / 2) / np.tan(np.radians(fov / 2))
    horizon = H / 2 + f * np.tan(np.radians(pitch))
    yawr = np.radians(yaw)
    zmax = zmax or w.N * w.res * 0.97
    vis = w.sun_shadow(sun)
    nt = normals_from(w.top, w.res)
    ng = normals_from(w.ground, w.res)
    out = {k: np.zeros((H, W)) for k in ('depth', 'sun', 'shadow', 'wx', 'wz', 'crown')}
    out['mat'] = np.full((H, W), SKY, int)
    out['nrm'] = np.zeros((H, W, 3)); out['nrm'][..., 1] = 1
    out['depth'][:] = np.inf
    out['crown'][:] = -1
    xs = np.arange(W)
    ang = yawr + np.arctan((xs - W / 2) / f)
    cosc = np.cos(np.arctan((xs - W / 2) / f))
    ybuf = np.full(W, H, float)
    d = 1.0
    ds = np.concatenate([np.arange(1, 60, 0.25), np.geomspace(60, zmax, 1400)])
    for d in ds:
        px = ex + np.sin(ang) * d / cosc * cosc   # along-ray world pos
        pz = ez + np.cos(ang) * d
        inside = (px > 0) & (px < w.N * w.res - 1) & (pz > 0) & (pz < w.N * w.res - 1)
        if not inside.any():
            continue
        top = w.sample(w.top, px, pz)
        lake = w.sample(w.lake.astype(float), px, pz) > 0.5
        if w.lake.any():
            top = np.where(lake, np.maximum(top, w.lake_level), top)
        dist = d  # perpendicular distance for projection
        sy = horizon - (top - ey) / dist * f
        sy = np.clip(sy, -1, H)
        draw = inside & (sy < ybuf)
        if not draw.any():
            continue
        for c in np.nonzero(draw)[0]:
            y0, y1 = int(np.ceil(max(sy[c], 0))), int(np.ceil(ybuf[c]))
            if y1 <= y0:
                continue
            out['depth'][y0:y1, c] = d / cosc[c]
            out['wx'][y0:y1, c] = px[c]; out['wz'][y0:y1, c] = pz[c]
        ybuf = np.where(draw, np.maximum(sy, 0), ybuf)
    # fill per-pixel attributes from world position
    m = np.isfinite(out['depth'])
    wx, wz = out['wx'][m], out['wz'][m]
    ix = np.clip((wx / w.res).astype(int), 0, w.N - 1); iz = np.clip((wz / w.res).astype(int), 0, w.N - 1)
    canopy = w.canopy[iz, ix] > 1.0
    mat = np.full(m.sum(), GRASS)
    mat[w.rock[iz, ix]] = ROCK
    mat[canopy & w.conifer_zone[iz, ix]] = CONIFER
    mat[canopy & ~w.conifer_zone[iz, ix]] = FOREST
    mat[w.stream[iz, ix] & ~canopy] = WATER
    mat[w.lake[iz, ix]] = WATER
    if w.snow:
        mat[(mat == GRASS) | (mat == ROCK)] = SNOW
    nn = np.where(canopy[:, None], nt[iz, ix], ng[iz, ix])
    nn[mat == WATER] = [0, 1, 0]
    out['mat'][m] = mat
    out['nrm'][m] = nn
    v = vis[iz, ix]
    out['shadow'][m] = 1 - v
    out['sun'][m] = np.clip(nn @ sun, 0, 1) * v
    out['crown'][m] = np.where(canopy, w.crown_id[iz, ix], -1)
    out['depth'][~m] = 1e6
    if far_ridges:
        _far_ridges(out, W, H, horizon, f, ey, seed)
    out['sun_dir'] = sun
    out['horizon'] = horizon
    return out


def _far_ridges(out, W, H, horizon, f, ey, seed):
    """layered silhouettes beyond the world: the far range (p.54). Each layer: a 1-D ridge profile."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed + 11)
    xs = np.arange(W)
    for dist, amp, base in [(22000, 1500, 400), (11000, 900, 300), (6000, 500, 250)]:
        prof = np.zeros(W)
        for s, a in [(W / 1.5, 1), (W / 4, .45), (W / 10, .2), (W / 30, .08)]:
            ph = rng.uniform(0, 2 * np.pi, 3)
            prof += a * (np.sin(xs / s * 2 + ph[0]) + 0.5 * np.sin(xs / s * 3.7 + ph[1]))
        prof = base + amp * (0.5 + 0.35 * prof)
        sy = horizon - (prof - ey) / dist * f
        for c in range(W):
            y0 = int(np.ceil(max(sy[c], 0)))
            col = out['mat'][:, c] == SKY
            rows = np.arange(H)
            sel = col & (rows >= y0)
            out['mat'][sel, c] = FAR
            out['depth'][sel, c] = dist
            # ridge normal: facing the viewer, tilted by profile slope
            g = np.gradient(prof)[c] * dist / f / 400
            nrm = np.array([-g, 0.5, -1.0]); nrm /= np.linalg.norm(nrm)
            out['nrm'][sel, c] = nrm
            out['sun'][sel, c] = 0.0

"""Oga's colour, as measured on his plates (sheets/00_plate_swatches.png) and as he describes it.

Ramps are paths in OKLCh. The measured facts they encode:

  greens (C-0081, C-0064, C-0007, p.44): hue walks from blue-green in the dark (h~150) to yellow in
      the light (h~108); chroma *rises* with lightness all the way to the top (C .02 at L .23,
      .17 at L .89). The lights are chrome-green/lemon, never whitened green. That is
      "unmuddied mixing": the mid-tones keep their chroma and the highlight is a vivid colour,
      not a value step toward white (p.10, step 10: "don't rely on value difference alone").
  shadows of stone (C-0792, p.56): a separate family, hue 250-266 (cool reflected sky), C .01-.03.
  greys (C39-5, p.103): tinted, two alternating tints (red-grey h~10, purple-grey h~320) plus an
      ochre accent; almost no neutral.
  snow (p.96): lit snow white; shadows lavender-blue h~285, C .03-.05.
"""
import numpy as np
from okl import lch_to_lab, oklab_to_rgb, rgb_to_oklab, lab_to_lch

D = np.pi / 180


def lch(L, C, hdeg):
    return lch_to_lab(np.array([L, C, hdeg * D], float))


def hue_lerp(h0, h1, t):
    d = (h1 - h0 + 180) % 360 - 180
    return h0 + d * t


def ramp_path(L0, L1, n, hue, chroma):
    """n colours from L0 to L1; hue(L) and chroma(L) are callables -> OKLab (n,3)."""
    Ls = np.linspace(L0, L1, n)
    return np.stack([lch(L, chroma(L), hue(L)) for L in Ls])


# ---- fitted to C-0081 (p.44): h = 160 - 58 L, C = 0.26 (L - 0.17), capped ------------------------
def oga_green(L0=0.26, L1=0.88, n=7, warm=0.0):
    return ramp_path(L0, L1, n, lambda L: 160 - 58 * L - 20 * warm,
                     lambda L: float(np.clip(0.26 * (L - 0.17), 0.012, 0.175)))


def mud_green(L0=0.26, L1=0.88, n=7):
    """the failure the book names: a mid green lightened with white, darkened with black."""
    mid = lch(0.55, 0.11, 125)
    out = []
    for L in np.linspace(L0, L1, n):
        t = (L - 0.55) / (1 - 0.55) if L > 0.55 else (0.55 - L) / 0.55
        tgt = lch(1.0, 0, 0) if L > 0.55 else lch(0.0, 0, 0)
        # mix in linear light, as paint (roughly) does
        a = rgb_to_lin_mix(mid, tgt, t)
        out.append(a)
    return np.stack(out)


def rgb_to_lin_mix(a, b, t):
    from okl import srgb_to_lin, lin_to_srgb
    ra, rb = srgb_to_lin(oklab_to_rgb(a)), srgb_to_lin(oklab_to_rgb(b))
    return rgb_to_oklab(lin_to_srgb(ra * (1 - t) + rb * t))


def linear_green(L0=0.26, L1=0.88, n=7):
    """a straight OKLab line between the same end colours as oga_green."""
    a, b = oga_green(L0, L1, 2)
    return np.stack([a * (1 - t) + b * t for t in np.linspace(0, 1, n)])


# ---- material palettes: lit family and shadow family, each a short ramp --------------------------
# Every family is written as (L, C, h) triplets, dark -> light. Values come from the plate swatches.
MATERIALS = {
    # meadow grass (C-0064/C-0007): lit yellow-greens, shadow deep blue-greens + a warm brown grey
    'grass':   dict(lit=[(0.62, 0.13, 122), (0.70, 0.145, 118), (0.79, 0.16, 112), (0.87, 0.165, 106)],
                    shadow=[(0.30, 0.035, 140), (0.37, 0.05, 136), (0.44, 0.07, 132), (0.50, 0.075, 128)],
                    warm=(0.42, 0.05, 80)),
    # broadleaf forest crowns (C-0064 trees)
    'forest':  dict(lit=[(0.55, 0.11, 125), (0.63, 0.13, 120), (0.72, 0.15, 114), (0.81, 0.16, 108)],
                    shadow=[(0.22, 0.02, 150), (0.28, 0.035, 145), (0.35, 0.05, 140), (0.42, 0.06, 135)],
                    warm=(0.36, 0.045, 70)),
    # conifers: darker, bluer, lower chroma (Oga's cedar "could be blacker", p.57)
    'conifer': dict(lit=[(0.44, 0.07, 140), (0.52, 0.09, 132), (0.60, 0.11, 126), (0.68, 0.12, 120)],
                    shadow=[(0.18, 0.015, 175), (0.23, 0.025, 165), (0.28, 0.035, 155), (0.34, 0.04, 148)],
                    warm=(0.30, 0.035, 60)),
    # rock (C-0792 / cover): lit warm grey, shadow cool blue-grey family
    'rock':    dict(lit=[(0.62, 0.02, 80), (0.70, 0.025, 75), (0.78, 0.028, 70), (0.86, 0.025, 70)],
                    shadow=[(0.24, 0.015, 255), (0.31, 0.02, 252), (0.38, 0.025, 250), (0.45, 0.03, 250)],
                    warm=(0.40, 0.03, 40)),
    'earth':   dict(lit=[(0.62, 0.06, 70), (0.70, 0.07, 72), (0.77, 0.075, 75), (0.84, 0.07, 80)],
                    shadow=[(0.33, 0.03, 50), (0.40, 0.035, 55), (0.46, 0.04, 60), (0.52, 0.04, 65)],
                    warm=(0.45, 0.05, 45)),
    'snow':    dict(lit=[(0.90, 0.012, 90), (0.93, 0.01, 95), (0.96, 0.006, 100), (0.985, 0.004, 100)],
                    # form shading of the snow (blue-grey) -- p.96
                    shadow=[(0.62, 0.035, 280), (0.68, 0.035, 283), (0.74, 0.032, 285), (0.80, 0.028, 285)],
                    # shadows cast ON the snow surface: celeste family
                    cast=[(0.64, 0.05, 250), (0.70, 0.05, 248), (0.76, 0.045, 245), (0.82, 0.04, 242)],
                    warm=(0.78, 0.02, 60)),
    'water':   dict(lit=[(0.62, 0.06, 225), (0.70, 0.06, 228), (0.78, 0.05, 230), (0.86, 0.04, 232)],
                    shadow=[(0.28, 0.04, 200), (0.34, 0.045, 205), (0.40, 0.05, 210), (0.46, 0.05, 215)],
                    warm=(0.40, 0.04, 150)),
}


def fam(m, key):
    return np.stack([lch(*t) for t in MATERIALS[m][key]])


def sky_ramp(hour='day'):
    """zenith -> horizon, near-isovalue walk (C-0772: celeste sky, pale at the horizon)."""
    if hour == 'day':
        return np.stack([lch(0.70, 0.11, 252), lch(0.75, 0.09, 250), lch(0.80, 0.07, 248),
                         lch(0.85, 0.05, 245), lch(0.90, 0.03, 240), lch(0.94, 0.02, 230)])
    raise ValueError(hour)
Write: books/oga/src/oga.py
"""The Oga method as a painter over fields.  (MODEL.md sections in brackets.)

paint(F, **params) -> dict(rgb=HxWx3, slot=HxW int, fam=HxW (0 shadow,1 lit), ...)

Passes, in the order Oga works (the cover, p.4-11):
  1  light plan [7]: one sun; light CONCENTRATED on the points (pool), most of the picture in shadow;
     contrast softened with distance [9, C-0007].
  2  ground [2]: the step field decided at cell resolution (the flat brush), per material, then
     laid with ragged wet edges (clumped-noise dithering between adjacent steps only).
  3  shadow colour [7,10]: shadow is its own family (cool reflected light on up-facing planes, the
     object's warm colour in patches, core darkest just past the terminator).
  4  air [11]: far things approach the sky colour; black point lifts with distance.
  5  edge of light [7, 際]: the light's colour where light meets the shadow edge.
  6  crowd / marks [3,5]: in the point areas only, lit leaf dabs on the sun side of crowns
     and tufts on near grass; "painting the unpainted" (C-0822).
"""
import numpy as np
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter, binary_dilation, uniform_filter
from okl import oklab_to_rgb
from palette import MATERIALS, fam, sky_ramp, lch
from ground import clumped_noise
import scene as S

MATKEY = {S.GRASS: 'grass', S.FOREST: 'forest', S.CONIFER: 'conifer', S.ROCK: 'rock',
          S.EARTH: 'earth', S.SNOW: 'snow', S.WATER: 'water', S.FAR: 'conifer'}

DEFAULTS = dict(
    points=[(0.62, 0.55, 0.22)],   # light pools: (x frac, y frac, radius frac)
    conc=0.6,                       # 0 = light everywhere the sun reaches; 1 = only in the pools
    terminator=0.30,                # sun value below which a surface is in the shadow family
    cell=3.0,                       # flat-brush width in px (organising size, near)
    rag=0.5,                        # raggedness of step boundaries (0 = clean bands)
    clump=1.3,                      # patch size of the ragged noise, px
    refl=0.8,                       # reflected light strength in shadow (sky-facing)
    warm_share=0.28,                # share of shadow area that takes the object's warm colour
    air=2600.0,                     # metres for aerial perspective to reach 63%
    air_max=0.85,
    edge=True,                      # light's colour at the edge of the light (際)
    edge_chroma=0.035,
    marks=True,
    seed=0,
    flat=False,                     # debug: plain lambert, no Oga (the 'before')
)


def organise(x, mask, sigma):
    """normalized gaussian blur of x inside mask (the painter organising a crowd into a shape)."""
    if sigma <= 0:
        return x
    w = gaussian_filter(mask.astype(float), sigma)
    v = gaussian_filter(np.where(mask, x, 0.0), sigma)
    return np.where(mask, v / np.maximum(w, 1e-6), x)


def pools(H, W, pts):
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W]
    p = np.zeros((H, W))
    for (px, py, r) in pts:
        p = np.maximum(p, np.exp(-(((xx - px * W) ** 2 + (yy - py * H) ** 2) / (r * W) ** 2)))
    return p


def paint(F, **kw):
    P = dict(DEFAULTS); P.update(kw)
    mat, depth, nrm = F['mat'], F['depth'], F['nrm']
    H, W = mat.shape
    sun = np.asarray(F['sun_dir'])
    s = F['sun'].copy()
    far = mat == S.FAR
    s[far] = np.clip(nrm[far] @ sun, 0, 1) * 0.8
    rng_seed = P['seed']

    # ---------- 1 light plan -------------------------------------------------------------------
    pool = pools(H, W, P['points'])
    s_eff = s * (1 - P['conc'] * (1 - pool)) if not P['flat'] else s
    # contrast softens with distance: lit and shadow converge toward the middle
    dfac = np.exp(-depth / (P['air'] * 1.2))
    s_eff = 0.5 + (s_eff - 0.5) * (0.35 + 0.65 * dfac) if not P['flat'] else s_eff

    # ---------- 2 ground: organise per material at cell size, scaled with depth -----------------
    x = np.zeros((H, W))
    if not P['flat']:
        for m in np.unique(mat):
            if m == S.SKY:
                continue
            mm = mat == m
            x = np.where(mm, organise(s_eff, mm, P['cell'] * 0.6), x)
    else:
        x = s_eff
    lit = x > P['terminator']

    # step coordinate in each family (0..3.999)
    t = P['terminator']
    lit_step = np.clip((x - t) / (1 - t) * 1.25, 0, 1) * 3.999
    ny = nrm[..., 1]
    back = np.clip(-(nrm @ sun), 0, 1)
    refl = np.clip(ny, 0, 1) * P['refl'] * (1 - 0.6 * back)
    core = np.clip(1 - np.abs(x - t * 0.6) / (t * 0.6 + 1e-6), 0, 1)   # darkest just past the terminator
    sh_step = np.clip(1.2 + 2.6 * refl - 1.4 * core, 0, 3.999)
    if P['flat']:
        sh_step = np.clip(x / t * 3.999, 0, 3.999)
    if not P['flat']:
        for m in np.unique(mat):
            if m == S.SKY:
                continue
            mm = mat == m
            sh_step = np.where(mm, organise(sh_step, mm, P['cell'] * 0.5), sh_step)
    step = np.where(lit, lit_step, sh_step)
    # ragged wet edges: dither between adjacent steps with clumped noise
    u = clumped_noise((H, W), P['clump'], rng_seed) - 0.5
    stepi = np.clip(np.floor(step + 0.5 + P['rag'] * u), 0, 3).astype(int)
    if P['flat']:
        stepi = np.clip(np.floor(step + 0.5), 0, 3).astype(int)

    # ---------- 3 colour: families, warm patches in shadow, snow cast family ------------------
    lab = np.zeros((H, W, 3))
    warmmask = clumped_noise((H, W), 4.0, rng_seed + 5) < P['warm_share']
    cast = F['shadow'] > 0.5
    for m in np.unique(mat):
        mm = mat == m
        if m == S.SKY:
            continue
        key = MATKEY[m]
        L_ = fam(key, 'lit'); Sh = fam(key, 'shadow')
        c = np.where(lit[..., None], L_[stepi], Sh[stepi])
        if not P['flat']:
            wc = lch(*MATERIALS[key]['warm'])
            wsel = mm & ~lit & warmmask & (stepi <= 2)
            c[wsel] = 0.5 * c[wsel] + 0.5 * (wc + (Sh[stepi[wsel]] - Sh[1]) * 0.6)
            if key == 'snow':
                Cc = fam('snow', 'cast')
                csel = mm & ~lit & cast
                c[csel] = Cc[stepi[csel]]
        lab[mm] = c[mm]
    # sky
    sky = mat == S.SKY
    sr = sky_ramp()
    hz = F.get('horizon', H * 0.4)
    yy = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W][0]
    sv = np.clip(yy / max(hz, 1), 0, 1) * (len(sr) - 1)
    svi = np.clip(np.floor(sv + 0.5 + 0.6 * u), 0, len(sr) - 1).astype(int)
    lab[sky] = sr[svi[sky]]

    # ---------- 4 air: far approaches the horizon sky colour ------------------------------------
    horizon_col = sr[-2]
    a = P['air_max'] * (1 - np.exp(-np.where(sky, 0, depth) / P['air']))
    if P['flat']:
        a = a * 0.5
    lab = lab * (1 - a[..., None]) + horizon_col * a[..., None]

    # ---------- 5 edge of light: 際 -------------------------------------------------------------
    if P['edge'] and not P['flat']:
        shad = ~lit & ~sky
        near_sh = binary_dilation(shad, iterations=1) & lit & ~sky
        e = near_sh & (depth < P['air'] * 1.5)
        L, A, B = lab[..., 0], lab[..., 1], lab[..., 2]
        C = np.hypot(A, B) + 1e-9
        hh = np.arctan2(B, A)
        target = 95 * np.pi / 180        # light's colour: warm yellow
        dh = (target - hh + np.pi) % (2 * np.pi) - np.pi
        hh2 = hh + 0.35 * dh
        C2 = C + P['edge_chroma']
        lab[e, 1] = (C2 * np.cos(hh2))[e]; lab[e, 2] = (C2 * np.sin(hh2))[e]

    return dict(rgb=oklab_to_rgb(lab), lab=lab, lit=lit, step=stepi, pool=pool, s_eff=s_eff)

    img=np.where(inside[...,None],oklab_to_rgb(rp[q]),oklab_to_rgb(lch(0.93,0.02,240))[None,None])
    sw=np.repeat(np.repeat(oklab_to_rgb(rp)[None],12,0),12,1)

"""Ex3 coloured greys (p.48, p.103 C39-5). Same values, three palettes: neutral greys, one tint,
Oga's alternating tints. Which reproduces his overcast plate?"""
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src')
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from okl import *
from palette import lch
from ground import clumped_noise
from sheet import *
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter
a=np.asarray(Image.open('plates/lv/C39-5_greys.png').convert('RGB'),float)/255
W=240; im=Image.fromarray(u8(a)); H=round(im.height*W/im.width)
small=np.asarray(im.resize((W,H),Image.BOX),float)/255
lab=rgb_to_oklab(small); L=lab[...,0]
Ls=np.quantile(L,np.linspace(0.03,0.97,9))
def pal_neutral(): return np.stack([lch(l,0.0,0) for l in Ls])
def pal_one(): return np.stack([lch(l,0.02,30) for l in Ls])
def pal_oga():
    # measured on the plate: red-grey h~10 and purple-grey h~320 alternate with value; an ochre sits high
    out=[]
    for i,l in enumerate(Ls):
        h=12 if i%2==0 else 320
        out.append(lch(l,0.022,h))
    out[6]=lch(Ls[6],0.05,63)   # the ochre (dry grass) step
    return np.stack(out)
def quant(pal,mode='value'):
    # value -> nearest two palette steps by L, dithered with clumped noise (organic)
    x=np.interp(L,Ls,np.arange(len(Ls)))
    u=clumped_noise(L.shape,1.2,3)-0.5
    q=np.clip(np.floor(x+0.5+0.6*u),0,len(Ls)-1).astype(int)
    return pal[q]
def quant_hue(pal_list):
    # choose, for each pixel, the palette entry nearest in full OKLab (value AND hue)
    P=np.concatenate(pal_list)
    d=((lab[...,None,:]-P[None,None])**2*np.array([2,1,1])).sum(-1)
    return P[np.argmin(d,-1)]
res={}
res['neutral greys (9 values)']=quant(pal_neutral())
res['one tint (warm grey)']=quant(pal_one())
res['Oga: alternating red/purple greys + ochre']=quant(pal_oga())
# a free choice: 9 neutral + 9 tinted, pixel picks nearest (does the plate WANT the tints?)
both=quant_hue([pal_neutral(),pal_oga()])
def blurde(x):
    return np.mean(np.linalg.norm(gaussian_filter(x,(1.5,1.5,0))-gaussian_filter(lab,(1.5,1.5,0)),axis=-1))
cells=[zoom(small,3)];labs=['his C39-5 (board, p.103), 240 px']
for k,v in res.items():
    cells.append(zoom(oklab_to_rgb(v),3)); labs.append(f'{k}   blurdE {100*blurde(v):.2f}')
cells.append(zoom(oklab_to_rgb(both),3))
Pn=pal_neutral(); share_t=np.mean(np.min(((both[...,None,:]-Pn[None,None])**2).sum(-1),-1)>1e-9)
labs.append(f'free choice neutral vs tinted: plate picks tinted for {100*share_t:.0f}% of pixels')
grid(cells,2,labs,title='Ex3  coloured greys: the same 9 values, different tints (C39-5, p.103)').save('sheets/ex3_greys.png')

# What Kazuo Oga teaches, in pixels: lessons

The book's model, in its own terms and with page references, is in [MODEL.md](MODEL.md). This file covers what I did with it: each principle translated into pixels, what carried and what didn't, and every dead end. Sheets are in `sheets/`, code in `src/`, and the exercise scripts in `work/`.

Scales: *close* is about 0.1 m/px, *landscape* is a 320×180 view from a person or a low hill, *iso* is a 30° parallel projection, *region* is about 1 m/px from above.

## 0. Before copying anything: calibrating the plates

- **The scan lies about black.** My first measurements said Oga keeps his darkest 2% at OKLab L 0.35, where photos sit at 0.20. That looked like a finding ("Oga lifts the black point") until I measured the black of the printed text on the same pages: L 0.29–0.35. It was the printing and scanning, not Oga. Every plate is now levels-corrected per page: black is the page's darkest 0.1%, white is the paper (`work/plates.py`, output in `plates/lv/`). After correction his black point (L 0.12–0.26) is like a photo's. **Measure the paper and the ink before you measure the painter.**
- **Global statistics barely separate Oga from photos** (`src/measure.py`: shadow share, chroma by value, tint count, detail concentration). Photos of the same subjects give numbers in the same ranges. What makes his pictures his is *where* things are put (which plane is lit, which is in shadow, where the detail is), not the histogram. So the measurements below are local, made inside one passage or on one kind of colour.
- **His pictures survive reduction to our size** (`sheets/00_survives.png`). C-0064, C-0007, C-0792 and the cover, area-reduced to 160 px wide, still read almost completely: the masses, the light and the space. Everything the reduction removes is his last layer. That is the book's claim about the ground stage (§2 of MODEL) seen from the other side, and it's why this translation is possible at all.

## 1. Colour: few paints, unmuddied, coloured greys

### 1a. The green ramp (C-0081, p.44) — `sheets/ex2_ramps.png`, `src/palette.py`

- **Measured:** 36 green clusters from C-0081, C-0064 and C-0007.
  - Hue walks from blue-green in the dark to yellow in the light, h ≈ 145 − 40·L.
  - Chroma *rises* almost linearly with value all the way to the top, C ≈ 0.27·(L − 0.18), capped at about 0.17.
  - His lightest green is lemon or chrome green, never a whitened green.
  - In C-0081 the mid-tones carry more chroma than a straight line between the ends: step 3 is C 0.10 against the line's 0.07, and step 4 is 0.13 against 0.10. These are the "bridging mid-tones" the book describes.
- **Pixel method:** a ramp is a path in OKLCh with hue(L) and chroma(L), not a line between two colours and never a colour plus white or black. `oga_green()` is the fitted path.
- **The failure the book names, reproduced:** the same mid green lightened with white and darkened with black. The lit side of the mound goes grey-white and the picture goes chalky. That's "mud" in pixel form: chroma falls as value rises.
- **Carries fully.** Pixel art already uses hue-shifted ramps. What Oga adds is the rule that chroma keeps rising into the light, and a measured slope for it.

### 1b. Coloured greys (C39-5, p.103) — `sheets/ex3_greys.png`, `sheets/ex3b_greys_by_place.png`

- **Measured on the overcast board:**
  - 92% of the pixels are low-chroma (C < 0.04).
  - They split into two tints that alternate: red-grey (h ≈ 10) and purple-grey (h ≈ 320).
  - One ochre (h 63) and one blue sit among them.
- **Dead end:** I tied the tints to value steps (red, purple, red, purple…) as in a pixel-art ramp. The ochre smeared across the sky, and the result was *worse* than neutral grey by eye, while the numbers barely moved (blurΔE 2.27 against neutral 2.34). **Tints belong to places, not to value steps**: ochre is the lit dry grass, red-grey the earth bank and trunks, purple-grey the sky and the far buildings.
- **What worked:** the same number of colours (24), with a red-grey and a purple-grey at each value, the lit ochre, and the one blue. Each pixel takes the nearest entry.
  - Tinted: blurΔE **1.37**. Neutral: **2.06**.
  - The tinted version reads as his picture; the neutral one reads as a photograph with the colour turned off.
- **Rule for us:** in low light, give every material its own tinted grey ramp: earth and wood warm, sky and distance violet. Put all the saturation of the picture in the one lit point. His grey world sings because of the orange patch the tanuki stands in.

## 2. The ground stage (地塗り)

### 2a. Reduction is not a ground stage — `sheets/ex1a_ground.png`, `src/ground.py`

- **What I tried:** a pixel "ground" made from an image. I chose *k* paints for the picture by k-means, decided them at the width of a flat brush (a 4–8 px cell), and never averaged two paints: each pixel is one paint. A soft weight field is sampled with a *clumped* noise, so paints bleed across boundaries in ragged tongues, and each mass is split into two "not fully mixed" halves in broad patches (塗りムラ).
- **First version: camouflage.** I sampled the soft weights everywhere, so every pixel was a coin toss and no mass had an identity. Fix: masses are decisive (weights raised to a power), bleeding happens only in a boundary band, and the unevenness is a separate, weak, broad split. The split amount has to stay small, about 0.012 in OKLab; at 0.03 the paper white splits into pink and blue.
- **The finding.** The same machine run on Oga's C-0064 gives a ground stage that reads as his painting. Run on a photo of the Rainier meadow, it gives camouflage. **A painting is already organised; a photo isn't.** Oga's ground stage fixes the light direction, the overall colour and the atmosphere (p.48). Those are decisions, and clustering doesn't make them.

### 2b. A ground from a plan — `sheets/ex1b_plan.png`, `src/plan.py`, `plans/rainier_valley.py`

- **What I did:** I drew an "art setting" for the photo: eight planes (sky, cliff, far forest, the two conifer stands, meadow, stream, path). Each plane gets exactly two paints, lit and shadow, and the lit/shadow shapes are decided at cell size and smoothed by one more cell, which is the painter organising the crowd.
- **Result:** the dark conifer masses, the lit meadow plane and the pale far slope read at once, and the picture has a structure the k-means version lacks.
- **Where it falls short:** my straight-edged polygons read as cut paper where they meet the sky. The plane boundary needs the same care as a mass outline: the conifer spires and the ragged treeline of the studio's earlier work.
- **Lesson:** the ground stage needs a plan with planes and one lit/shadow decision per plane. voxsim can supply the plan, since it knows the material, the depth and the normal of every pixel. So from here on I paint from simulated fields (`src/scene.py`), not from photos, and use the photos only as references.
Write: books/oga/src/boulders.py
"""Close-scale boulder field (the gorge of C-0792, p.56) rendered to fields by ray casting.

Each boulder is an ellipsoid cut by a few joint planes (convex), so it has faces and edges, as rock
does. The gorge: a back wall (rising plane), a water surface, and a LIGHT WINDOW: the sun reaches the
floor only through a gap between the canyon rims, a band in world space. Most of the picture is in
the wall's shadow; light falls on the few boulder planes that face the sun inside the band.
"""
import numpy as np
from scipy.spatial.transform import Rotation as R

ROCK, WATER, WALL, SKY = 2, 6, 10, 0


def make_boulders(n=26, seed=0, spread=(9.0, 14.0)):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    bs = []
    for i in range(n):
        z = rng.uniform(2.5, spread[1])
        x = rng.uniform(-spread[0], spread[0]) * (0.4 + z / spread[1])
        s = rng.uniform(0.7, 2.0) * (0.8 + z / 20)
        radii = s * np.array([rng.uniform(0.9, 1.5), rng.uniform(0.55, 0.85), rng.uniform(0.8, 1.2)])
        rot = R.from_euler('xyz', rng.uniform(-0.35, 0.35, 3) * [1, 3, 1]).as_matrix()
        c = np.array([x, radii[1] * 0.55, z])
        cuts = []
        for k in range(rng.integers(2, 5)):
            nrm = rng.standard_normal(3); nrm[1] = abs(nrm[1]) * 0.6 + 0.2; nrm /= np.linalg.norm(nrm)
            cuts.append((nrm, rng.uniform(0.55, 0.85)))      # plane: n.(p-c) <= d * |radii along n|
        bs.append((c, radii, rot, cuts))
    return bs


def render(W=200, H=110, seed=0, eye=(0, 2.2, -2.0), look=(0, 0.6, 8.0), fov=62,
           sun=(-0.55, 0.62, 0.55), window=(-2.0, 3.5), wall_z=15.0, water_y=0.15):
    sun = np.asarray(sun, float); sun /= np.linalg.norm(sun)
    eye = np.asarray(eye, float); fwd = np.asarray(look, float) - eye; fwd /= np.linalg.norm(fwd)
    right = np.cross([0, 1, 0], fwd); right /= np.linalg.norm(right); up = np.cross(fwd, right)
    f = (W / 2) / np.tan(np.radians(fov / 2))
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W]
    d = (fwd[None, None] * f + right[None, None] * (xx - W / 2)[..., None] - up[None, None] * (yy - H / 2)[..., None])
    d /= np.linalg.norm(d, axis=-1, keepdims=True)
    D = d.reshape(-1, 3); O = np.broadcast_to(eye, D.shape)
    bs = make_boulders(seed=seed)
    t, nrm, bid = hit_all(O, D, bs)
    mat = np.full(len(D), SKY)
    # back wall: plane z = wall_z, leaning back
    tw = (wall_z - O[:, 2]) / np.where(np.abs(D[:, 2]) > 1e-6, D[:, 2], 1e-6)
    tw = np.where(tw > 0, tw, np.inf)
    # water plane
    tq = (water_y - O[:, 1]) / np.where(np.abs(D[:, 1]) > 1e-6, D[:, 1], 1e-6)
    tq = np.where(tq > 0, tq, np.inf)
    tt = np.minimum(np.minimum(t, tw), tq)
    mat[tt == t] = ROCK
    mat[(tt == tw) & (tw < np.inf)] = WALL
    mat[(tt == tq) & (tq < tw)] = WATER
    N = np.zeros_like(D); N[:, 1] = 1
    N[mat == ROCK] = nrm[mat == ROCK]
    wn = np.array([0, 0.25, -1.0]); wn /= np.linalg.norm(wn)
    N[mat == WALL] = wn
    P = O + D * np.where(np.isfinite(tt), tt, 0)[:, None]
    # light: window band on the ground plane, projected along the sun; plus boulder cast shadows
    proj = P - sun[None] * (P[:, 1:2] / sun[1])          # where the sun ray through P hits y=0
    inwin = (proj[:, 0] > window[0]) & (proj[:, 0] < window[1])
    inwin &= mat != WALL
    occl = np.zeros(len(D), bool)
    so = P + N * 1e-3
    t2, _, _ = hit_all(so[inwin], np.broadcast_to(sun, (inwin.sum(), 3)), bs)
    occl[np.nonzero(inwin)[0][np.isfinite(t2)]] = True
    lightv = inwin & ~occl
    ndl = np.clip(N @ sun, 0, 1)
    out = dict(mat=mat.reshape(H, W), nrm=N.reshape(H, W, 3), depth=np.where(np.isfinite(tt), tt, 1e3).reshape(H, W),
               sun=(ndl * lightv).reshape(H, W), ndl=ndl.reshape(H, W), window=lightv.reshape(H, W),
               bid=np.where(mat == ROCK, bid, -1).reshape(H, W), pos=P.reshape(H, W, 3), sun_dir=sun)
    return out


def hit_all(O, D, bs):
    n = len(D)
    best = np.full(n, np.inf); nb = np.zeros((n, 3)); bid = np.full(n, -1)
    for i, (c, radii, rot, cuts) in enumerate(bs):
        # to local ellipsoid space
        o = (O - c) @ rot; dd = D @ rot
        os, ds = o / radii, dd / radii
        a = (ds * ds).sum(1); b = 2 * (os * ds).sum(1); cc = (os * os).sum(1) - 1
        disc = b * b - 4 * a * cc
        ok = disc > 0
        sq = np.sqrt(np.where(ok, disc, 0))
        t0 = (-b - sq) / (2 * a); t1 = (-b + sq) / (2 * a)
        tin = np.where(ok, t0, np.inf); tout = np.where(ok, t1, -np.inf)
        p_in = os + ds * tin[:, None]
        nin = p_in / radii; nsrc = nin.copy()             # ellipsoid normal (local)
        for (pn, dd_) in cuts:
            pnl = pn @ rot                                  # plane normal in local coords
            ext = dd_ * np.sqrt(((pnl * radii) ** 2).sum())
            den = dd @ pnl; num = ext - o @ pnl
            tp = num / np.where(np.abs(den) > 1e-9, den, 1e-9)
            entering = den < 0
            upd = entering & (tp > tin)
            tin = np.where(upd, tp, tin)
            nsrc[upd] = pnl
            tout = np.where(~entering & (tp < tout), tp, tout)
            # ray parallel & outside: kill
            tout = np.where((np.abs(den) <= 1e-9) & (num < 0), -np.inf, tout)
        valid = (tin < tout) & (tin > 1e-4) & (tin < best)
        best = np.where(valid, tin, best)
        nw = nsrc @ rot.T
        nw /= np.linalg.norm(nw, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-12
        nb[valid] = nw[valid]; bid[valid] = i
    return best, nb, bid
Bash: cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/oga && cat > work/ex4.py <<'EOF' """Ex4 light concentrated, shadow the majority, colour in the shadow (C-0792, p.56).""" import sys; sys.path.inser
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/oga && cat > work/ex4.py <<'EOF'
"""Ex4 light concentrated, shadow the majority, colour in the shadow (C-0792, p.56)."""
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src')
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from okl import *
from palette import lch, fam
from ground import clumped_noise
from oga import organise
from scipy.ndimage import binary_dilation, gaussian_filter
import boulders as B
from sheet import *

def paint_gorge(F, mode='oga', seed=0, cell=2.0, rag=0.55):
    mat,N,ndl,sun=F['mat'],F['nrm'],F['ndl'],F['sun']
    H,W=mat.shape; lab=np.zeros((H,W,3))
    lit_f=fam('rock','lit'); sh_f=fam('rock','shadow')
    full=np.concatenate([sh_f,lit_f])                 # 8 steps dark->light
    u=clumped_noise((H,W),1.2,seed)-0.5
    rock=mat==B.ROCK
    if mode=='before':
        # every boulder lit by the same sun, lambert through the whole ramp
        x=np.clip(0.15+0.85*ndl,0,1)*7.999
        q=np.clip(np.floor(x+0.5),0,7).astype(int)
        lab[rock]=full[q][rock]
    else:
        lit=(sun>0.22)&rock
        # organise per boulder: faces become planes
        s=np.where(lit,ndl,0)
        for b in np.unique(F['bid'][rock]):
            m=F['bid']==b
            s=np.where(m,organise(s,m&lit,cell*0.5),s)
        lstep=np.clip((s-0.22)/0.6,0,1)*3.999
        ny=N[...,1]
        tow=np.clip(N@np.array([0.7,0.2,-0.5]),0,1)       # faces turned to the lit floor get bounce
        pos=F['pos']; low=np.clip(1-pos[...,1]/0.5,0,1)      # contact: dark near the ground
        sstep=np.clip(0.9+2.2*np.clip(ny,0,1)+0.8*tow-1.6*low,0,3.999)
        for b in np.unique(F['bid'][rock]):
            m=(F['bid']==b)&~lit
            sstep=np.where(m,organise(sstep,m,cell*0.6),sstep)
        step=np.where(lit,lstep,sstep)
        q=np.clip(np.floor(step+0.5+rag*u),0,3).astype(int)
        c=np.where(lit[...,None],lit_f[q],sh_f[q])
        # the rock's own warm colour in the shadow, in patches (world-anchored would be better)
        warm=lch(0.40,0.035,45)
        wm=(clumped_noise((H,W),3.0,seed+4)<0.22)&rock&~lit&(q>=1)
        c[wm]=0.55*c[wm]+0.45*(warm+(sh_f[q[wm]]-sh_f[1])*0.8)
        # the edge of the light (際): lit face pixels touching shadow get the light's colour
        edge=binary_dilation(rock&~lit)&lit
        cl=lab_to_lch(c[edge]); cl[:,1]+=0.03; cl[:,2]=cl[:,2]*0.6+np.radians(85)*0.4
        c[edge]=lch_to_lab(cl)
        lab[rock]=c[rock]
    # wall: dark, in shadow, cool with warm streaks (same in both, so the comparison is fair)
    wall=mat==B.WALL
    vs=clumped_noise((H,W),1.0,seed+9)
    vs=gaussian_filter(vs,(3,0.4)); vs=(vs-vs.min())/(vs.max()-vs.min())
    wq=np.clip((vs*3.2+0.3*u).astype(int),0,3)
    wall_f=np.stack([lch(0.20,0.02,240),lch(0.25,0.025,235),lch(0.30,0.03,60),lch(0.36,0.03,200)])
    if mode=='before': wall_f=np.stack([lch(0.30+0.08*i,0.02,70) for i in range(4)])
    lab[wall]=wall_f[wq][wall]
    wat=mat==B.WATER
    wf=np.stack([lch(0.24,0.03,220),lch(0.30,0.035,215),lch(0.40,0.04,210),lch(0.62,0.05,215)])
    wq=np.clip((F['window']*2.5+1+0.8*u).astype(int),0,3) if mode!='before' else np.full((H,W),2)
    lab[wat]=wf[wq][wat]
    lab[mat==B.SKY]=lch(0.8,0.04,220)
    return oklab_to_rgb(lab)

`vox-app --headless --size 1024x1024x192 --seed 1`, 1 tick = 1 sim-minute.

## Phase 0 — starting facts (from ~/work/voxsim/runs/e2e2/run.log, before any change)

- 72/72 dead by tick 3500 (day 2.4). Clothed 72 at init -> 41 at t100 -> 5 at t500.
- Air at people: -2.6 .. 9.3 C over the run (spring, dry hill country); the
  per-tick spread across all people is ~0.2 C (temperature is sea-level temp
  minus lapse rate only; no local microclimate). Rain ~0.
- thirst>=0.75 in 16 people by t500 (8 h), hunger>=0.75 in 10 by t600.
- Physiology in code (exec.rs person_tick): thirst 0->1 in 16 h, then health
  -1/2880 per min (2 days); cold: comfort = air + 8 clothed + 14 fire + 10 home
  + 8 carried + 3/huddler - 4*rain, cold rises (12-comfort)/900 per min, at 1
  health -1/2880 per min; hunger 0->1 in 24 h then 14 days. Drains add.
- Offline random decider: uniform category, then uniform option.

## Phase 1 — instrumentation (in progress)

Added (vox-human): `Stats.surv` counters (frames of thirsty / cold-and-bare /
hungry people and whether they offered drink / put-on / make-clothing /
fire / eat), clothing-verb choice counts, drinks that found no water, a
per-death line (`[human] death ...`: stage, worn, carried clothing, needs,
hours since last drink, nearest water, parents alive, task), and the
survival line no longer prints +-3.4e38 when nobody is alive.

(random, except people serve bodily needs and never undress/drop clothing).
Runs started: `base` (random, instrumented), `oracle0` (needs-oracle, no mechanics changes).

### Phase 1 measurements from `base` (random decider, OLD mechanics, instrumented; partial, t~1600)

- Water is reachable: 6666/6746 (99%) frames of people with thirst>=0.75
  offered a drink option; at death the nearest water was 0-15 m away
  (mostly 0-2 m). Hungry frames offered eating 12032/12033.
- Random picks bathe (1191) twice as often as any drink verb (drink_from 608,
  drink_here 130, drink_at_place 56), and 72% of drink_from tasks were
  interrupted by the next decision before arriving (437/608). Several children
  died having **never** drunk in 26-29 h, with water 0-2 m away.
  -> thirst deaths are the random decider (plus random interrupts), not reachability.
- Clothing flow: take_off 141 vs put_on 69; drop_item 70, give 35 (of those
  involving clothing). Dropped items were DELETED (no ground item) and the
  dead's clothing/belongings vanished with them -> mechanics bug.
- cold_bare frames (cold>=0.75 and unclothed): put_on offered only 9% (only
  when carrying clothing), a fire option 2%.
- Cold model (old) had no dependence on how cold, wind, wet, activity, body
  size; clothed people at -2 C were "very cold" and on a 2-day death clock.

def render_iso(w, x0, z0, width_m, mpp=0.5, elev=30.0, sun=(0.6, 0.55, -0.55), H=None):
    """30-degree parallel projection looking north (+z) over a patch [x0, x0+width_m] x [z0, ...].
    screen x = world x; screen y = (z - z0) * sin(e) down->up ... drawn front to back with a y-buffer."""
    sun = np.asarray(sun, float); sun /= np.linalg.norm(sun)
    e = np.radians(elev); se, ce = np.sin(e), np.cos(e)
    W = int(width_m / mpp); H = H or int(W * 9 / 16)
    vis = w.sun_shadow(sun, steps=120, step=1.0)
    nt = normals_from(w.top, w.res); ng = normals_from(w.ground, w.res)
    xs = x0 + (np.arange(W) + 0.5) * mpp
    out = {k: np.zeros((H, W)) for k in ('depth', 'sun', 'shadow', 'wx', 'wz', 'crown')}
    out['mat'] = np.full((H, W), SKY, int); out['nrm'] = np.zeros((H, W, 3)); out['nrm'][..., 1] = 1
    out['crown'][:] = -1
    h0 = w.sample(w.ground, np.array([x0 + width_m / 2]), np.array([z0]))[0]
    ybuf = np.full(W, float(H))
    zmax = z0 + (H * mpp) / se + 80
    for zz in np.arange(z0 - 40, zmax, mpp * se * 0.8):
        top = w.sample(w.top, xs, np.full(W, zz))
        sy = H - ((zz - z0) * se + (top - h0) * ce) / mpp
        for c in np.nonzero(sy < ybuf)[0]:
            y0, y1 = int(np.ceil(max(sy[c], 0))), int(np.ceil(min(ybuf[c], H)))
            if y1 > y0:
                out['wx'][y0:y1, c] = xs[c]; out['wz'][y0:y1, c] = zz
                out['depth'][y0:y1, c] = (zz - z0) * ce - (top[c] - h0) * se + 100
        ybuf = np.minimum(ybuf, np.maximum(sy, 0))
    m = out['wz'] != 0
    wx, wz = out['wx'][m], out['wz'][m]
    ix = np.clip((wx / w.res).astype(int), 0, w.N - 1); iz = np.clip((wz / w.res).astype(int), 0, w.N - 1)
    canopy = w.canopy[iz, ix] > 1.0
    mat = np.full(m.sum(), GRASS)
    mat[w.rock[iz, ix]] = ROCK
    mat[canopy & w.conifer_zone[iz, ix]] = CONIFER
    mat[canopy & ~w.conifer_zone[iz, ix]] = FOREST
    mat[w.stream[iz, ix] & ~canopy] = WATER
    mat[w.lake[iz, ix]] = WATER
    if w.snow:
        mat[(mat == GRASS) | (mat == ROCK)] = SNOW
    nn = np.where(canopy[:, None], nt[iz, ix], ng[iz, ix]); nn[mat == WATER] = [0, 1, 0]
    out['mat'][m] = mat; out['nrm'][m] = nn
    v = vis[iz, ix]; out['shadow'][m] = 1 - v
    out['sun'][m] = np.clip(nn @ sun, 0, 1) * v
    out['crown'][m] = np.where(canopy, w.crown_id[iz, ix], -1)
    out['depth'][~m] = 1e6
    out['sun_dir'] = sun; out['horizon'] = 0
    return out
'''
open('src/scene.py','w').write(s)
EOF

# gridref.py plate W scale out   : area-reduce plate to W px wide, show at scale with labelled 8px grid

from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
from sheet import FONT_S

Write: books/oga/src/copykit.py
"""copykit: deliberate pixel placement for master copies of Oga.

A Canvas holds palette indices. Everything is placed by the copyist: polygons for masses
(the flat brush), ragged 'wet' edges, and small brush marks (dabs) at chosen positions.
Nothing here decides *where*; the copy script does, by eye from the gridded plate.

Brush vocabulary, from the book:
  mass      flat brush area (地塗り); optional wet edge (にじみ): the boundary is broken into a
            1-2 px band of tongues instead of a cut
  flat_stagger  the flat brush shifted vertically in steps (cover step 6): vertical dashes of
            random length, staggered, along a ridge: conifers on a far peak
  splay     the splayed flat brush (バサつかせ, step 7): a fan of 1-px ticks: shrub silhouettes
  dab       a leaf shape of the sakuyo tip: tiny glyphs (1x1..3x2) in a lit colour
  line      clean 1-px run (branches, the blue rim of light on a rock edge)
  blade     grass: 1-px run with a lean, lit tip
"""
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter, binary_dilation, binary_erosion


class Canvas:
    def __init__(self, W, H, pal, bg=0, seed=0):
        self.W, self.H = W, H
        self.pal = np.asarray(pal, float)          # sRGB 0..1, (n,3)
        self.a = np.full((H, W), bg, np.int16)
        self.rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
        self.stages = []

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- utilities
    def mask_poly(self, pts, ss=4):
        im = Image.new('L', (self.W * ss, self.H * ss), 0)
        ImageDraw.Draw(im).polygon([(x * ss, y * ss) for x, y in pts], fill=255)
        return np.asarray(im.resize((self.W, self.H), Image.BOX)) > 127

    def noise(self, scale=1.2, seed=None):
        r = np.random.default_rng(self.rng.integers(1 << 30) if seed is None else seed)
        n = gaussian_filter(r.standard_normal((self.H, self.W)), scale)
        return (n - n.mean()) / (n.std() + 1e-9)

    def snapshot(self, name):
        self.stages.append((name, self.a.copy()))

    def rgb(self, a=None):
        return self.pal[self.a if a is None else a]

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- masses
    def mass(self, pts_or_mask, idx, wet=0, wet_scale=0.9, where=None):
        """fill a mass. wet>0: the edge is ragged by `wet` px (bleeding tongues)."""
        m = self.mask_poly(pts_or_mask) if not isinstance(pts_or_mask, np.ndarray) else pts_or_mask
        if wet > 0:
            n = self.noise(wet_scale)
            from scipy.ndimage import distance_transform_edt as edt
            din = edt(m); dout = edt(~m)
            sd = np.where(m, din, -dout)                  # signed distance, + inside
            m = sd + wet * 0.8 * n > 0.5
        if where is not None:
            m &= where
        self.a[m] = idx
        return m

    def split(self, mask, p0, p1, idx_side, wet=0.8):
        """paint the part of `mask` on the left of the line p0->p1 with idx_side (a lit plane or
        shadow plane of a rock): planes are decided by a drawn line, not by a normal."""
        yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:self.H, 0:self.W]
        (x0, y0), (x1, y1) = p0, p1
        side = (x1 - x0) * (yy - y0) - (y1 - y0) * (xx - x0)
        side = side / (np.hypot(x1 - x0, y1 - y0) + 1e-9)
        if wet:
            side = side + wet * self.noise(0.8)
        m = mask & (side < 0)
        self.a[m] = idx_side
        return m

    def mottle(self, mask, idx, share=0.25, scale=1.6):
        """塗りムラ: broad uneven patches of a second paint inside a mass."""
        n = self.noise(scale)
        thr = np.quantile(n[mask], 1 - share) if mask.any() else 0
        m = mask & (n > thr)
        self.a[m] = idx
        return m

    def gradient(self, mask, idxs, axis_pts, rag=0.6, scale=1.0):
        """a hake gradation across a mass through the listed paints (adjacent steps interleaved)."""
        yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:self.H, 0:self.W]
        (x0, y0), (x1, y1) = axis_pts
        d = np.array([x1 - x0, y1 - y0], float); L2 = (d ** 2).sum()
        t = ((xx - x0) * d[0] + (yy - y0) * d[1]) / L2
        t = np.clip(t, 0, 0.9999) * len(idxs)
        q = np.clip(np.floor(t + rag * self.noise(scale) * 0.5), 0, len(idxs) - 1).astype(int)
        self.a[mask] = np.asarray(idxs)[q][mask]

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- marks
    def put(self, x, y, idx):
        x, y = int(round(x)), int(round(y))
        if 0 <= x < self.W and 0 <= y < self.H:
            self.a[y, x] = idx

    def line(self, pts, idx, width=1):
        """clean 1-px runs through the listed points (Bresenham-like, no anti-aliasing)."""
        for (x0, y0), (x1, y1) in zip(pts[:-1], pts[1:]):
            n = int(max(abs(x1 - x0), abs(y1 - y0))) + 1
            for t in np.linspace(0, 1, n + 1):
                x = x0 + (x1 - x0) * t; y = y0 + (y1 - y0) * t
                self.put(x, y, idx)
                if width > 1:
                    self.put(x, y + 1, idx)

    DABS = {
        'dot': [(0, 0)], 'h2': [(0, 0), (1, 0)], 'v2': [(0, 0), (0, 1)],
        'leaf': [(0, 0), (1, 0), (0, 1)], 'leafr': [(0, 0), (1, 0), (1, 1)],
        'blob': [(0, 0), (1, 0), (0, 1), (1, 1)], 'h3': [(-1, 0), (0, 0), (1, 0)],
        'drop': [(0, -1), (-1, 0), (0, 0), (1, 0)], 'diag': [(0, 0), (1, 1)], 'adiag': [(1, 0), (0, 1)],
    }

    def dab(self, x, y, idx, shape='leaf', where=None):
        for dx, dy in self.DABS[shape]:
            X, Y = int(round(x + dx)), int(round(y + dy))
            if 0 <= X < self.W and 0 <= Y < self.H and (where is None or where[Y, X]):
                self.a[Y, X] = idx

    def scatter_dabs(self, region, idx, n, shapes=('leaf', 'leafr', 'dot', 'h2'), bias=None, seed=None):
        """place n dabs inside region; bias: array of weights (e.g. toward the lit side)."""
        r = np.random.default_rng(self.rng.integers(1 << 30) if seed is None else seed)
        ys, xs = np.nonzero(region)
        if len(ys) == 0:
            return
        w = np.ones(len(ys)) if bias is None else np.clip(bias[ys, xs], 0, None) + 1e-9
        pick = r.choice(len(ys), size=min(n, len(ys)), replace=False, p=w / w.sum())
        for i in pick:
            self.dab(xs[i], ys[i], idx, shapes[r.integers(len(shapes))], where=region)

    def flat_stagger(self, ridge, idx, lens=(1, 4), width=1, spacing=1, seed=None, down=True):
        """cover step 6: flat brush shifted vertically -> staggered vertical dashes hanging from a
        ridge line (list of (x,y)); models conifers on a far peak."""
        r = np.random.default_rng(self.rng.integers(1 << 30) if seed is None else seed)
        xs = np.arange(min(p[0] for p in ridge), max(p[0] for p in ridge) + 1, spacing)
        rx = np.array([p[0] for p in ridge], float); ry = np.array([p[1] for p in ridge], float)
        for x in xs:
            y0 = np.interp(x, rx, ry) + r.integers(-1, 2)
            L = r.integers(lens[0], lens[1] + 1)
            for k in range(L):
                for w in range(width):
                    self.put(x + w, y0 + (k if down else -k), idx)

    def splay(self, x, y, idx, n=4, length=3, spread=1.2, angle=-90, seed=None):
        """splayed flat brush tip: a fan of short 1-px ticks from (x,y)."""
        r = np.random.default_rng(self.rng.integers(1 << 30) if seed is None else seed)
        for i in range(n):
            a = np.radians(angle + (i - (n - 1) / 2) * spread * 12 + r.normal(0, 6))
            L = length * r.uniform(0.6, 1.1)
            for t in np.arange(0, L, 0.7):
                self.put(x + np.cos(a) * t, y + np.sin(a) * t, idx)

    def blade(self, x, y, idx, length=5, lean=0.3, tip=None):
        for k in range(length):
            self.put(x + lean * k, y - k, idx)
        if tip is not None:
            self.put(x + lean * (length - 1), y - length + 1, tip)


def sample_colors(img_small, pts):
    """average the 3x3 around each (x,y) of an area-reduced reference -> sRGB 0..1."""
    a = np.asarray(img_small.convert('RGB'), float) / 255
    out = []
    for x, y in pts:
        out.append(a[max(0, y - 1):y + 2, max(0, x - 1):x + 2].reshape(-1, 3).mean(0))
    return np.array(out)
