Write: books/oga/copies/p5_gorge.py
"""Passage copy P5: C-0792 (p.56), light cut into a gorge of boulders, 142x77. Painted by hand.

The book's point for this plate: shadow is about 2/3 of the picture, which makes the light dazzle;
light falls only on certain planes; don't spread light around, concentrate it. Three light levels:
direct sun (brightest), soft light, reflected light in shadow (dim but important). By day the
reflected light takes the sky's colour (cool); loosen it with the object's own colour (moss greens,
rock browns).

Carbon transfer: the boulder outlines are my polygons read off copies/p5_grid.png (his layout);
every plane's light is my decision following the book's text; no pixel is sampled from the plate.
"""
import sys
ROOT = __file__.rsplit('/', 2)[0]
sys.path.insert(0, ROOT + '/src')
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from okl import lch_to_lab, oklab_to_rgb
from copykit import Canvas
import vocab as V


def c(L, C, h):
    return oklab_to_rgb(lch_to_lab(np.array([L, C, np.radians(h)])))


P = dict(
    void=c(0.14, 0.015, 220), v2=c(0.19, 0.02, 230), v3=c(0.24, 0.03, 240),          # the chasm: blue-black
    sh1=c(0.26, 0.025, 250), sh2=c(0.32, 0.03, 255), sh3=c(0.38, 0.035, 258),        # rock in shadow
    ms1=c(0.30, 0.04, 150), ms2=c(0.37, 0.05, 140),                                   # moss in shadow (object colour)
    rf1=c(0.46, 0.10, 268), rf2=c(0.56, 0.12, 265),                                   # reflected sky light: violet-blue
    so1=c(0.58, 0.03, 200), so2=c(0.66, 0.025, 210),                                  # soft light
    li1=c(0.74, 0.03, 85), li2=c(0.83, 0.03, 80), li3=c(0.93, 0.02, 90),              # direct sun
    edge=c(0.80, 0.08, 95),                                                           # the light's colour at its edge
    leaf=c(0.62, 0.12, 150), leaf2=c(0.48, 0.09, 155), wat=c(0.30, 0.03, 200),
)
NAMES = list(P); I = {k: i for i, k in enumerate(NAMES)}; PAL = np.array([P[k] for k in NAMES])
NXT = {'sh1': 'sh2', 'sh2': 'sh1', 'sh3': 'sh2', 'li1': 'li2', 'li2': 'li1', 'li3': 'li2', 'so1': 'so2', 'ms1': 'ms2', 'so2': 'so1'}


def paint(seed=5):
    W, H = 142, 77
    cv = Canvas(W, H, PAL, bg=I['void'], seed=seed)
    # ground: the chasm, blue-black, uneven; far lit leaves top-left; a water glimpse
    cv.mottle(np.ones((H, W), bool), I['v2'], 0.35, 3.0)
    cv.mottle(np.ones((H, W), bool), I['v3'], 0.12, 2.5)
    lv = cv.mass([(24, 0), (50, 0), (48, 14), (40, 20), (28, 18)], I['leaf2'], wet=1.5)
    cv.mottle(lv, I['leaf'], 0.45, 1.2)
    cv.mass([(56, 58), (76, 56), (80, 62), (60, 64)], I['wat'], wet=0.8)
    cv.snapshot('1 the chasm: blue-black, uneven; far lit leaves; a water glimpse')
    ix = I
    # shadowed boulders first (the majority), each: shadow body, moss patch, sky-light rim on top
    shadowed = [
        ([(0, 0), (30, 0), (26, 24), (12, 28), (0, 30)], [([(0, 0), (30, 0), (28, 6), (0, 8)], 'sh2')]),
        ([(84, 42), (100, 34), (118, 32), (126, 40), (120, 54), (100, 58), (86, 54)], [([(84, 42), (100, 34), (118, 32), (122, 36), (100, 40), (86, 46)], 'sh3'),
                                                                                    ([(92, 48), (112, 44), (118, 52), (100, 56)], 'ms1')]),
        ([(112, 12), (124, 10), (134, 14), (138, 28), (128, 38), (116, 36), (110, 24)], [([(112, 12), (124, 10), (134, 14), (126, 16), (114, 18)], 'sh3')]),
        ([(60, 20), (74, 16), (84, 20), (86, 30), (74, 34), (62, 30)], [([(66, 22), (80, 20), (82, 26), (70, 28)], 'ms1')]),
        ([(126, 44), (142, 40), (142, 66), (128, 62)], []),
        ([(118, 58), (132, 56), (142, 60), (142, 77), (116, 77)], [([(118, 58), (132, 56), (142, 60), (142, 63), (120, 62)], 'sh3')]),
    ]
    for outline, faces in shadowed:
        m = V.facet_rock(cv, outline, faces, ix, 'sh1', NXT)
        # reflected light: the up-facing top edge takes the sky's colour (violet-blue), 1-2 px
        top = m & ~np.roll(m, 1, 0)
        cv.a[top] = I['rf2']
        cv.a[m & ~np.roll(m, 2, 0) & ~top] = I['rf1']
    cv.snapshot('2 boulders in shadow: blue-grey, moss patches, the sky-coloured reflected light on tops')
    # lit boulders: direct sun on their top planes only; faces in shadow; the edge of the light
    lit = [
        ([(0, 34), (10, 28), (20, 28), (30, 36), (38, 50), (42, 62), (38, 66), (24, 58), (10, 48), (0, 44)],
         [([(0, 34), (10, 28), (20, 28), (30, 36), (38, 50), (40, 58), (30, 50), (16, 38), (4, 38)], 'li2'),
          ([(8, 30), (18, 30), (26, 38), (18, 36)], 'li3')]),
        ([(34, 24), (44, 22), (54, 28), (56, 34), (46, 36), (36, 32)], [([(34, 24), (44, 22), (54, 28), (48, 30), (38, 28)], 'li2')]),
        ([(40, 44), (52, 42), (62, 44), (64, 50), (60, 56), (46, 56), (40, 52)], [([(40, 44), (52, 42), (62, 44), (63, 48), (50, 48), (42, 49)], 'li3'),
                                                                                 ([(44, 49), (60, 48), (62, 51), (48, 53)], 'li1')]),
        ([(76, 64), (94, 60), (110, 62), (114, 72), (110, 77), (74, 77)], [([(76, 64), (94, 60), (110, 62), (104, 66), (84, 68)], 'li1')]),
        ([(0, 50), (14, 52), (26, 62), (32, 72), (30, 77), (0, 77)], [([(0, 50), (14, 52), (26, 62), (28, 68), (14, 62), (0, 58)], 'so2'),
                                                                     ([(0, 58), (14, 62), (28, 68), (30, 77), (0, 77)], 'so1')]),
    ]
    for outline, faces in lit:
        m = V.facet_rock(cv, outline, faces, ix, 'sh2', NXT)
        # the edge of the light: lit-plane pixels bordering the shadow face take the light's colour
        litp = m & np.isin(cv.a, [I['li1'], I['li2'], I['li3']])
        shp = m & ~litp
        border = litp & (np.roll(shp, -1, 0) | np.roll(shp, 1, 1) | np.roll(shp, -1, 1))
        cv.a[border] = I['edge']
    cv.snapshot('3 light only on certain planes: sun on the tops, the light-colour edge; the rest in shadow')
    # detail lives in the light: a few crack marks on the lit planes; lichen dots
    for pts in ([(10, 34), (18, 40), (24, 46)], [(46, 45), (52, 46)], [(84, 64), (92, 63)], [(4, 38), (8, 42)]):
        cv.line(pts, I['li1'])
    cv.snapshot('4 detail in the light only')
    return cv

Write: books/oga/places/olycoast.py
"""Place study: the Olympic coast, driftwood on the gravel at Rialto Beach under a coastal forest. 320x172.
Hand-designed. Photo: places/olycoast_photo.jpg ('Driftwood along Rialto Beach 2022', Sea Cow, CC BY-SA 4.0).
(v1 used the generic painter: a pale blob for the root, uniform gravel, a flat forest wall. See LESSONS.)

Oga's decisions:
  [4] overcast: the light comes from the whole sky, i.e. from ABOVE. So every form is lit on its top
      planes and dark underneath: cream tops on the driftwood, cool grey-green undersides, a soft cool
      cast shadow on the gravel right under each log. Coloured greys everywhere (C39-5, p.103).
  [7] the point is the root mass: the only near-white and the only strong dark-light contact.
  [6] planes: sky (two values) / the forest wall with spruce spires and bleached snags breaking it /
      the salal band + the back log line (the severing band) / the gravel plane / the driftwood.
  [3] gravel as a crowd: flat far, dots mid, 2-px stones near, each stone lit on top (overcast).
"""
import sys
ROOT = __file__.rsplit('/', 2)[0]
sys.path.insert(0, ROOT + '/src')
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter
from okl import lch_to_lab, oklab_to_rgb
from copykit import Canvas, majority
import vocab as V

W, H = 320, 172


def c(L, C, h):
    return oklab_to_rgb(lch_to_lab(np.array([L, C, np.radians(h)])))


P = dict(
    sk1=c(0.80, 0.012, 300), sk2=c(0.88, 0.01, 280),
    f1=c(0.26, 0.03, 175), f2=c(0.33, 0.035, 165), f3=c(0.41, 0.04, 155), f4=c(0.50, 0.04, 150), fa=c(0.58, 0.025, 200),
    sn1=c(0.74, 0.012, 60), sn2=c(0.62, 0.015, 60),
    sa1=c(0.44, 0.07, 130), sa2=c(0.56, 0.09, 125), sa3=c(0.34, 0.05, 135),
    g0=c(0.66, 0.012, 70), g1=c(0.58, 0.014, 65), g2=c(0.50, 0.015, 60), g3=c(0.42, 0.015, 280), g4=c(0.34, 0.015, 270),
    gl=c(0.74, 0.012, 75), gw=c(0.55, 0.03, 50),
    w1=c(0.92, 0.02, 80), w2=c(0.84, 0.025, 75), w3=c(0.72, 0.025, 65), w4=c(0.58, 0.02, 180), w5=c(0.44, 0.02, 200), w6=c(0.32, 0.02, 220),
    cs=c(0.36, 0.02, 250),
)
NAMES = list(P); I = {k: i for i, k in enumerate(NAMES)}; PAL = np.array([P[k] for k in NAMES])


def log(cv, pts, r0, r1, seed=0):
    """a driftwood log along a polyline: radius r0 -> r1. Overcast: top 1/3 cream, middle grey-warm,
    underside cool dark; a soft cool cast shadow on the ground under it."""
    rr = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    pts = np.array(pts, float)
    segs = []
    for (a, b) in zip(pts[:-1], pts[1:]):
        n = int(np.hypot(*(b - a))) + 1
        for t in np.linspace(0, 1, n):
            segs.append(a + (b - a) * t)
    segs = np.array(segs)
    rad = np.linspace(r0, r1, len(segs))
    # cast shadow first (below, shifted down 1-2 px)
    for (x, y), r in zip(segs, rad):
        for dx in range(-1, 2):
            for dy in range(int(r), int(r) + 3):
                cv.put(x + dx, y + dy, I['cs'])
    for (x, y), r in zip(segs, rad):
        for dy in range(-int(round(r)), int(round(r)) + 1):
            f = dy / max(r, 0.5)                 # -1 top .. +1 bottom
            idx = I['w1'] if f < -0.45 else (I['w2'] if f < -0.1 else (I['w3'] if f < 0.35 else (I['w4'] if f < 0.7 else I['w5'])))
            cv.put(x, y + dy, idx)
    # grain: a few long 1-px lines of the next darker step along the log
    for k in range(int(len(segs) / 12)):
        i = rr.integers(0, max(1, len(segs) - 8))
        for j in range(int(rr.integers(4, 9))):
            x, y = segs[min(i + j, len(segs) - 1)]
            cv.put(x, y + rr.integers(-1, 1), I['w3'])


def paint(seed=21):
    cv = Canvas(W, H, PAL, bg=I['sk1'], seed=seed)
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W]
    r = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    # ---- sky: two values
    cv.a[yy > 30 + 4 * cv.noise(4.0)] = I['sk2']
    # ---- the forest wall: a mass; top edge = spires of varying height; snags; overcast top-lit tiers
    FOREST_TOP = lambda x: 50 + 6 * np.sin(x / 23.0) + 4 * np.sin(x / 7.0 + 1)
    fb = 92
    forest = (yy >= FOREST_TOP(xx)) & (yy < fb)
    cv.a[forest] = I['f2']
    cv.mottle(forest, I['f1'], 0.35, 2.2)
    # far pale trees at the right end (haze toward the sea)
    haze = forest & (xx > 290)
    cv.a[haze] = I['fa']
    # spires breaking the sky; tall ones where the photo has the big spruces
    tall = {78: 60, 96: 44, 120: 40, 238: 56, 252: 46, 30: 36, 180: 34, 300: 30}
    for x in range(0, W, 5):
        x_ = x + r.integers(-2, 3)
        h = tall.get(min(tall, key=lambda k: abs(k - x_)), 16) if min(abs(k - x_) for k in tall) < 4 else int(r.integers(10, 24))
        base = FOREST_TOP(x_) + h * 0.5
        V.spire(cv, x_, base, h, {'b': I['f2'], 'd': I['f1'], 'l': I['f3']}, body='b', dark='d', lit='l', sun=0 or 1, w=max(1.5, h * 0.16), seed=x_)
    # overcast: the top of each tier catches the sky -> a light fleck on tier tops inside the forest
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(forest)
    for i in r.choice(len(ys), size=len(ys) // 30, replace=False):
        cv.put(xs[i], ys[i], I['f3']); cv.put(xs[i] + 1, ys[i], I['f3'])
    # bleached snags at the left
    for (x, y0, y1) in [(24, 30, 90), (30, 40, 90), (36, 48, 90), (44, 36, 90), (52, 54, 90), (12, 50, 90)]:
        cv.line([(x, y0), (x + 1, y1)], I['sn1'])
        for k in range(y0, y1, 5):
            cv.line([(x, k), (x + (2 if k % 10 else -2), k - 2)], I['sn2'])
    cv.snapshot('1 sky (two values), forest wall: spires breaking the sky, snags, overcast tier tops')
    # ---- the salal band: clumps lit on top; the back driftwood line along the forest foot
    band = (yy >= 84) & (yy < 100 + 2 * cv.noise(3.0))
    cv.a[band] = I['sa3']
    for x in range(0, W, 4):
        y = 88 + r.integers(-2, 5)
        V.clump(cv, x + r.uniform(-1, 1), y, r.uniform(2.0, 3.2), 'sa2', 'sa1', 'sa3', I, seed=x)
    for pts in ([(0, 99), (40, 98), (70, 100)], [(150, 101), (210, 99), (260, 100)], [(270, 97), (320, 96)], [(80, 102), (130, 101)]):
        log(cv, pts, 1.0, 0.8, seed=pts[0][0])
    cv.snapshot('2 salal band (clumps lit on top), the back log line')
    # ---- the gravel plane: flat far, dots mid, stones near; overcast: stones lit on top
    gravel = (yy >= 100 + 2 * cv.noise(3.0)) & ~band
    gravel |= yy >= 104
    t = np.clip((yy - 100) / (H - 100), 0, 1)
    cv.a[gravel] = I['g1']
    cv.a[gravel & (t < 0.25)] = I['g0']
    cv.mottle(gravel & (t > 0.2), I['g2'], 0.35, 3.0)
    cv.a[gravel & (xx < 70) & (t < 0.3) & (cv.noise(2.0) > 0.3)] = I['gw']     # the wet brown patch at left
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(gravel & (t > 0.18))
    for i in r.choice(len(ys), size=int(len(ys) * 0.22), replace=False):
        x, y = xs[i], ys[i]
        tt = t[y, x]
        w = 1 + int(tt * 2.6 * r.uniform(0.5, 1.2))
        if tt < 0.45:
            cv.put(x, y, I['g3'] if r.uniform() < 0.5 else I['gl']); continue
        for k in range(w):
            cv.put(x + k, y, I['gl'] if r.uniform() < 0.8 else I['g0'])
            cv.put(x + k, y + 1, I['g4'])
    cv.snapshot('3 gravel: flat far, dots mid, 2-3 px stones near, lit tops')
    # ---- the driftwood: the log, the root mass (the point), a small log
    log(cv, [(112, 140), (140, 132), (170, 126), (204, 118)], 4.2, 3.2, seed=1)
    log(cv, [(56, 152), (72, 146), (90, 141)], 2.2, 1.8, seed=2)
    root = cv.mask_poly([(204, 104), (222, 98), (244, 100), (262, 106), (270, 122), (266, 146), (252, 156), (230, 152), (212, 140), (204, 122)])
    # cast shadow of the root on the gravel (cool, soft, right under it)
    cs = cv.mask_poly([(206, 142), (232, 154), (254, 158), (272, 150), (276, 160), (230, 164), (204, 152)])
    cv.a[cs & ~root] = I['cs']
    # the root mass as facets: overcast = cream top planes, grey-warm faces, cool dark underside pockets
    cv.a[root] = I['w3']
    cv.mottle(root, I['w4'], 0.35, 1.8)
    top = root & (yy < 112 + 0.25 * (xx - 204))
    cv.a[top] = I['w2']; cv.mottle(top, I['w1'], 0.4, 1.4)
    under = root & (yy > 136 + 0.1 * (xx - 204))
    cv.a[under] = I['w5']; cv.mottle(under, I['w6'], 0.35, 1.2)
    for pts in ([(214, 116), (224, 128), (230, 140)], [(236, 110), (242, 126), (246, 146)], [(252, 112), (258, 130)]):
        cv.line(pts, I['w6'])                                   # crevices between the root folds
    # root spikes fanning up and left from the mass, lit on top
    for (x0, y0, x1, y1) in [(206, 106, 188, 88), (212, 102, 204, 80), (220, 100, 218, 78), (226, 99, 232, 84),
                             (206, 112, 180, 104), (208, 108, 190, 96)]:
        cv.line([(x0, y0), (x1, y1)], I['w2'])
        cv.line([(x0 + 1, y0 + 1), (x1 + 1, y1 + 1)], I['w4'])
    cv.snapshot('4 driftwood: logs (cream tops, cool undersides, cast shadow), the root mass as the point')
    return cv

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/books/oga && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('src/vocab.py').read()
a=s.index("def spire("); b=s.index("def conifer_mass(")
s=s[:a]+'''def spire(cv, x, yb, h, ix, body='c_mid', dark='c_dark', lit='c_lit', sun=+1, w=None, seed=0, where=None):
    """one conifer: base at (x, yb), height h px. Built as the book's staggered flat-brush dabs: a 1-px
    leader, then branch tiers at irregular 2-4 row intervals, each tier a short drooping dash whose
    length varies left/right; the sun side (sun=+1 right, -1 left, 0 overcast = tops) gets lit ends."""
    r = _rng(seed)
    w = w if w is not None else max(1.0, h * 0.22)
    top = int(round(yb - h))
    x = float(x)
    y = top
    cv_put = lambda X, Y, idx: (where is None or (0 <= Y < cv.H and 0 <= X < cv.W and where[int(Y), int(round(X))])) and cv.put(X, Y, idx)
    # leader
    for yy in range(top, int(yb) + 1):
        cv_put(x, yy, ix[body])
    cv_put(x, top, ix[dark])
    y = top + int(r.integers(1, 3))
    while y <= yb:
        t = (y - top) / max(h, 1)
        for side in (-1, 1):
            L = w * t * r.uniform(0.6, 1.35) + 0.5
            n = int(round(L))
            for k in range(1, n + 1):
                droop = 1 if k > 0.6 * n and n >= 3 else 0
                X = x + side * k
                idx = ix[body]
                if side * (sun if sun else 1) < 0 and sun != 0:
                    idx = ix[dark]
                if k == n and (side == sun or (sun == 0)):
                    idx = ix[lit]
                cv_put(X, y + droop, idx)
                if k < n * 0.5:
                    cv_put(X, y + 1, ix[dark] if side * (sun or 1) < 0 else ix[body])
        y += int(r.integers(2, 4))
    # fill the body between tiers near the trunk so the tree reads as a mass, not a ladder
    for yy in range(top + 2, int(yb)):
        t = (yy - top) / max(h, 1)
        for k in range(1, int(w * t * 0.55) + 1):
            for side in (-1, 1):
                cv_put(x + side * k, yy, ix[dark] if (side * (sun or 1) < 0 and sun != 0) else ix[body])


'''+s[b:]
open('src/vocab.py','w').write(s)

Write: books/oga/places/lane_skykomish.py
"""The method applied to a voxsim render: the place-skykomish lane's Index wall (renders/sky/wall_pair_o
right half), repainted the Oga way at 320x193. No photo: the render is the only source.

What the render gives: rock (grey), conifers (green), their shadows. What it lacks, by the book:
  - one concentrated light (every rock face is lit the same; dark streaks everywhere) [7]
  - shadow colour: its shadows are neutral near-black [4][10]
  - organisation: trees are scattered members, not a crowd with a mass [3]
  - a severing band and an order of planes [6]
Passes:
  1 masses from the render (rock / trees), organised (smoothed 2 px);
  2 light: a cloud shadow over the left 60% of the wall, following the fall line; lit granite = the
    render's lightest organised values, shadowed granite = a simple cool family with warm loosening;
  3 trees: a dark crowd with spires only where the render has a tree top against rock, lit tips on the
    sun side (left);
  4 the edge of the light: the light's colour where the cloud edge crosses lit rock.
"""
import sys
ROOT = __file__.rsplit('/', 2)[0]
sys.path.insert(0, ROOT + '/src')
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter, binary_dilation, label
from okl import rgb_to_oklab, lch_to_lab, oklab_to_rgb
from copykit import Canvas, majority
import vocab as V

W = 320


def c(L, C, h):
    return oklab_to_rgb(lch_to_lab(np.array([L, C, np.radians(h)])))


P = dict(g1=c(0.88, 0.018, 85), g2=c(0.78, 0.02, 80), g3=c(0.67, 0.022, 70), rim=c(0.74, 0.10, 245),
         q1=c(0.62, 0.025, 255), q2=c(0.54, 0.028, 258), q3=c(0.45, 0.025, 255), qw=c(0.56, 0.022, 55),
         c_lit=c(0.56, 0.09, 135), c_mid=c(0.43, 0.07, 145), c_dark=c(0.31, 0.045, 160), c_hole=c(0.21, 0.03, 170),
         s_mid=c(0.36, 0.05, 185), s_dark=c(0.27, 0.04, 195))
NAMES = list(P); I = {k: i for i, k in enumerate(NAMES)}; PAL = np.array([P[k] for k in NAMES])


def paint(seed=51):
    src = Image.open(f'{ROOT}/places/skykomish_render.png').convert('RGB')
    H = round(src.height * W / src.width)
    a = np.asarray(src.resize((W, H), Image.BOX), float) / 255
    lab = rgb_to_oklab(a)
    L = gaussian_filter(lab[..., 0], 1.0)
    C = np.hypot(lab[..., 1], lab[..., 2]); hue = np.degrees(np.arctan2(lab[..., 2], lab[..., 1])) % 360
    tree = gaussian_filter(((hue > 100) & (hue < 200) & (C > 0.03)).astype(float), 1.2) > 0.45
    rock = ~tree
    cv = Canvas(W, H, PAL, seed=seed)
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W]
    # 2 light: the cloud shadow, following the fall line
    cloud = (xx - 0.3 * yy + 8 * cv.noise(3.0)) < 175
    Lr = gaussian_filter(lab[..., 0], 2.0)
    q = np.quantile(Lr[rock], [0.25, 0.55, 0.85])
    lev = np.searchsorted(q, Lr)
    lit_names = np.array([I['q3'], I['g3'], I['g2'], I['g1']])
    sh_names = np.array([I['q3'], I['q2'], I['q2'], I['q1']])
    cv.a[rock & ~cloud] = lit_names[lev][rock & ~cloud]
    cv.a[rock & cloud] = sh_names[lev][rock & cloud]
    cv.a = majority(cv.a, 3)
    cv.mottle(rock & cloud & (cv.a == I['q1']), I['qw'], 0.3, 2.0)
    litm = rock & ~cloud & np.isin(cv.a, [I['g1'], I['g2']])
    dk = np.roll(np.isin(cv.a, [I['q2'], I['q3']]), -1, 0)
    r = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    cv.a[litm & dk & (r.uniform(size=(H, W)) < 0.6)] = I['rim']
    cedge = rock & ~cloud & binary_dilation(cloud & rock) & np.isin(cv.a, [I['g2'], I['g3']])
    cv.a[cedge] = I['g1']
    V.fall_strokes(cv, litm, 'g3', I, n=60, lens=(2, 7), drift=0.05, seed=seed)
    cv.snapshot('1-2 rock: organised values, one concentrated light (cloud shadow on the left 60%)')
    # 3 trees: a dark crowd; spires where a tree mass has its top against rock
    cv.a[tree] = I['c_hole']
    Lt = gaussian_filter(lab[..., 0], 1.0)
    cv.a[tree & (Lt > np.quantile(Lt[tree], 0.5))] = I['c_dark']
    cv.a[tree & cloud & (cv.a == I['c_dark'])] = I['s_dark']
    lab_, n_ = label(tree)
    tops = tree & ~np.roll(tree, 2, 0)
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(tops)
    done = set()
    for i in r.permutation(len(ys)):
        x, y = xs[i], ys[i]
        key = (x // 4, y // 6)
        if key in done:
            continue
        done.add(key)
        h = int(r.integers(7, 15))
        lit = not cloud[y, x]
        V.spire(cv, x, y + h * 0.8, h, I, body='c_mid' if lit else 's_mid', dark='c_dark' if lit else 's_dark',
                lit='c_lit' if lit else 'c_mid', sun=-1, seed=int(x * 7 + y), where=tree | binary_dilation(tree, iterations=2))
    cv.snapshot('3 trees: a dark crowd, spires where a tree top meets the rock, lit tips (sun left) out of the cloud')
    return cv, a


if __name__ == '__main__':
    from sheet import zoom, grid, fit
    cv, a = paint()
    Image.fromarray((cv.rgb() * 255 + .5).astype(np.uint8)).save(f'{ROOT}/places/out/lane_skykomish.png')
    photo = Image.open(f'{ROOT}/places/skykomish_photo.jpg')
    grid([fit(photo, w=640), zoom(a, 2), zoom(cv.rgb(), 2)], 1,
         ['photo (Index Town Walls, SounderBruce, CC BY-SA 4.0)', 'before: the place-skykomish lane render, area-reduced to 320 px', 'after: the same render repainted by the Oga passes'],
         title='The method on a voxsim render: Skykomish, the Index wall').save(f'{ROOT}/sheets/lane_skykomish.png')

### 4e. P4: the grass slope (C-0064) — `copies/p4_meadow.py`, `sheets/p4_meadow.png`

- **What his slope is made of:**
  - one plane with an undulation (C-0095, p.53);
  - a broad lemon crest band where it faces the sun, turning through mid green into a dark base;
  - strokes as tapered wedges that lean *along the slope's flow*;
  - contrast inside the mark: darker strokes in the lit band, lighter blades in the dark band, and big dark blade silhouettes at the base;
  - band boundaries broken by blade tips, never a line.
- **Pixel method:**
  1. The value turn is a function of distance below the crest line, modulated a little by his organised values. That alone is stage 1, and it already reads as a slope.
  2. Wedge strokes: 2 px wide at the base, 1 px after, about 28° lean, one step off the local band.
  3. Tips over the crest.
  4. Dark silhouettes at the base.
  5. Flowers as points.
- **First pass:** too busy in the lit crest. His crest is calm, so strokes there are halved (density at the points, p.58).
- **Carries well at 2×.** This is the meadow mark I reuse.

### 4f. P5: light cut into a gorge (C-0792) — `copies/p5_gorge.py`, `sheets/p5_gorge.png`

- **The book's point:** shadow about 2/3; light only on certain planes; three light levels. Reflected light takes the sky's colour, loosened by the object's own colour.
- **Built by hand:**
  1. A blue-black chasm.
  2. Shadowed boulders with moss patches (the object's colour in shadow).
  3. Lit boulders with sun only on their top planes and a 1-px band of the light's colour where the lit plane meets its own shadow.
  4. Detail (cracks) only in the light.
- **Measured:** 14% of pixels are in the light families.
- **Dead end:** I first drew the reflected sky light as a 1–2 px rim on every shadowed boulder. It read as a cartoon outline. In his plate the reflected light is a *plane*: the upper third of each up-facing shadowed boulder, broken by the rock's colour. With that change the gorge fills with his violet-blue.
- **What doesn't carry:** his wet-in-wet softness inside the lit planes. At this size I use one or two steps plus uneven paint.

## 5. The earlier procedural exercise, re-read

`sheets/ex4_gorge.png` (the raycast boulders lit only through a gap) comes to the same conclusion as P5 by another route. Lambert light on every stone gives "a pile of cookies". Letting the light in through a gap puts about 2/3 of the rock in shadow, and the picture gets a subject. It's the one procedural result I kept, because it's a *light decision*, not a texture.

## 6. The places: painting the five the way Oga would

Each study is a script in `places/`. It states Oga's decisions at the top, section by section of MODEL.md, then paints stage by stage (`sheets/place_*_stages.png`).

- **Masses:** hand polygons, or where that is too crude, the photo's own shapes smoothed. That's the "carbon transfer" of the art setting.
- **Light:** always my decision: a cloud shadow, a gap, overcast, or backlight.
- **Marks:** the passage vocabulary (`src/vocab.py`).

Photo credits are in each script and in `places/refs/*/sources.tsv`. One sheet shows everything: `sheets/99_places.png`.

### What happened, in order

- **Skykomish** (`places/skykomish.py`), the Index wall.
  - v1 drew the wall as vertical facets from my imagination and read as a striped curtain.
  - v2 took the wall's planes from the photo's values, organised. Better, but a cold critic said: "nothing says where the sun is, and light and shadow cover about the same area; two identical chartreuse domes steal the eye."
  - v3:
    - a cloud shadow over the left 60% of the wall, its edge following the fall line;
    - lit granite on the right buttresses only, with the light's colour where the cloud edge crosses it;
    - the shadowed granite simplified to three cool steps with a warm loosening;
    - one near maple, mostly in shadow.

  The light now makes the picture. Still weak: the ridge forest band is flat.
- **The generic painter.** For Enchantments, the coast and Yakima I first wrote `src/painter.py`: a Scene of masses plus one function per material, with colour families built by Oga's rules from one key colour.
  - It worked for **Enchantments**: a reflection that mirrors the peak one step down, the clear bed in the near water, skylight on the tops of shadowed boulders, and the larch gold as the only saturated note.
  - It failed the other two. On **the coast** the root mass became "a pale camouflage blob … a crashed plane" and the gravel was uniform. On **Yakima** it drew rectangular cloud boxes and mirrored the hills in a river 30 m wide.

  That was the Genge failure again at a smaller scale: a general machine gives general pictures. I rewrote both by hand.
- **Yakima by hand** (`places/yakima.py`).
  - My ruled geometry read as "a striped flag" and the willow band as fence panels.
  - What fixed it:
    - the photo's own light shape for the hills;
    - the willows as a crowd of tall clumps lit from the upper left, with the gold grass showing between;
    - fence posts without a ruled line;
    - a short mirror of the band in the river;
    - the drift boat as the one point.
  - Still weak: the top-left sky and far ridge, and a pine that reads as a stick.
- **The coast by hand** (`places/olycoast.py`).
  - **Overcast means light from above:** every form is lit on its top plane and dark underneath, and gets a cool soft cast shadow right under it.
  - The forest was improved by a better conifer mark (below).
  - Gravel runs from flat far, to dots, to 2–3 px stones lit on top near.
  - **The root plate still doesn't read.** It's a brown disc with cream folds and radiating roots, like a cake or an umbrella. The photo's root tangle is the hardest subject in the set. At 60 px wide it wants to be drawn root by root, which is exactly what the book says not to do with a crowd. I haven't found its organised impression yet. That's an honest open problem.
- **San Juan** (`places/sanjuan.py`) came out strongest. The critic: "the only one where one light and colourful shadow make up the picture."
  - Backlit oaks as violet masses, with sky holes only near the sun and orange rims of the light at the edges facing it.
  - The meadow glows far and darkens near. The oaks' shadows are cast toward us as dark tongues.
  - The camas are one mark: tiny and dense far, big and sparse near.
  - The mistake: I first scattered the camas uniformly, "one identical stamp in even rows". The fix is the book's crowd principle: **organise the crowd into drifts.** The drift field is smooth noise sampled in *ground* coordinates, so patches are round on the ground and flatten with distance. The camas become a violet sea with gaps.

### A better conifer mark

The first `spire` drew regular tiers of equal width, cartoon Christmas trees. The fix is the cover's step 6 in pixels:
- a 1-px leader;
- branch tiers at irregular 2–4 row intervals, each a short dash of random length left and right, with drooping ends;
- the lit end on the sun side, or on top when overcast;
- the core filled near the trunk so it reads as a mass, not a ladder.

At 4× the coastal forest now reads as spruce against a grey sky.

### The method on a voxsim render (`places/lane_skykomish.py`, `sheets/lane_skykomish.png`)

- **Input:** the place-skykomish lane's render of the same wall, and nothing else.
- **Passes:**
  1. masses from the render (rock and trees by hue);
  2. one light (a cloud shadow on 60%, the lit granite from the render's lightest organised values, the shadow a simple cool family);
  3. trees as a dark crowd, with spires only where a tree top meets rock.
- **Result:** the render's even lighting and neutral black shadows become a picture with a lit buttress and coloured shadow.
- **Still weak:** the render has no sky, no ridge and no foreground, so there's no severing band. Oga's planes can't be invented by a repaint; the lane's camera and composition have to supply them.

## 7. What didn't carry, and why

- **Wet-in-wet** (にじみ, ぼかし). Pixels have no water. I can imitate a bleed's *edge* with ragged tongues and its *unevenness* with broad patches of the adjacent step, but not the soft gradation inside a wash. At 320 px that is mostly fine: his gradations become two or three flat steps with wet seams.
- **The airbrush** (moonlight in the air, C-1482). Its whole point is diffuse light, and there is no pixel equivalent without dithering noise. Use the brush alternative the book gives: light made by shadows cast between trees (C-1486).
- **Brush tempo.** "Know beforehand what shape each brush motion makes" carries as *a mark per material*. The improvised tempo does not carry. Randomness imitates variety, not rhythm; the rhythm has to be designed (tier spacing, drift fields, the alternation of branches).
- **Global statistics.** Coloured greys, a bridging mid-range and concentrated detail don't separate his plates from photos when measured over the whole image. What makes a picture his is *where*: the decisions per plane. Measure locally or not at all.
- **Photos as a source of light.** Photos have their own light, usually spread everywhere. Every study that took its light from the photo came out flat. The light has to be designed per picture: a cloud, a gap, overcast top-light, backlight.

## 8. The rules, for students and the voxsim lanes

These are the book's model reduced to what a pixel painter can apply. Each rule has a passage copy as its reference image and regression test.

1. **Ground first** (地塗り). Decide the masses, one light, and the colour families at the lowest detail. The picture must read at this stage "by its silhouette alone". Detail is a thin last layer that must not break it.
   - *Test:* stage 1 of any `*_stages.png` sheet should already look like the picture.
2. **One light, concentrated** (C-0792). About 60–85% of the area is in shadow. Light falls on certain planes only: the ones facing it, where the world lets it through (a gap, a cloud break, the sky from above when overcast).
   - Never a screen-space spotlight (my first try, `src/oga.py`, did that).
   - *Reference:* `copies/p5_gorge.py`.
3. **Shadow is full of colour.**
   - Faces turned up take the sky's colour as a plane (violet-blue by day). Faces turned down stay darkest. The object's own warm colour is loosened in, in patches.
   - The shadow side is kept simple: three steps, no detail.
   - *Reference:* P3, P5.
4. **The light's colour at its edge** (際). Where a lit plane meets its own shadow, or a silhouette meets the glow, draw a 1-px band of the light's colour: warm by sun, sky-blue on the far edge of a lit rock top, orange at dusk.
   - *Reference:* P3's cobalt rim, San Juan's crown rims.
5. **Ramps.** Hue walks toward yellow as value rises, and chroma *rises* into the light: C ≈ 0.27·(L − 0.18) for greens, capped at 0.17. Never lighten with white or darken with black.
   - Greys are always tinted, and tints belong to places (earth warm, sky and distance violet), not to value steps.
   - Code: `src/palette.py`, `painter.palette_for()`. Measured: `sheets/ex2_ramps.png`, `ex3b`.
6. **A mark per material** (`src/vocab.py`). Know the shape before painting the crowd:
   - broadleaf: rounded clumps at four light levels (P1);
   - conifer: leader plus irregular drooping tiers (the cover's staggered flat brush);
   - far forest: staggered vertical dashes in irregular groups (P2);
   - rock: facets decided by lines, uneven paint inside one step (P3);
   - grass: wedges along the slope's flow, contrast inside the mark (P4);
   - flowers: one spike, organised into drifts (San Juan).
7. **Organise the crowd** (群れを捉える). Members are never scattered uniformly. They sit on the organised values of the mass, or in drifts sampled in ground coordinates, with gaps. About half of a crown mass is hole.
8. **Density only at the points.** Choose one to three points per picture (the lit buttress, the larch band, the boat, the root). Detail goes there; the rest holds at ground level. Lit crests stay calm.
9. **Sever to make depth.** A band of shadow, a willow band or a shore of pale rock between the near and the far. The far is sky-tinted and one step flatter; contrast softens with distance.
10. **Water mirrors what is painted above it, one step down.** Near the viewer you look *into* the water and see the bed. Dash rows of sky colour only in the mirror part. A thin light line where water meets the shore.

### Code

- `src/copykit.py` is the canvas of palette indices, with masses, wet edges, uneven paint, lines, dabs and the majority clean-up.
- `src/vocab.py` holds the marks, each with named palette slots and parameters (sizes, levels, spacing, sun side).
- `src/palette.py` holds the measured ramps. `painter.palette_for(key)` builds a lit family, a shadow family and a warm variant from one key colour.
- `src/painter.py` is the generic Scene painter over masses (form source plus materials plus designed light). **Use it only for a first ground.** My place studies show it gets the ground and the palette right and the marks wrong, unless the marks are placed by hand.
- `copies/*.py` are the passage copies, which are the references and regression tests. `places/*.py` are the five place studies and the lane repaint.
- The procedural ground experiments (`src/ground.py`, `src/scene.py`, `src/oga.py`) are kept as the honest record of §2–3.
